			</item>
		<item>
		<title>Build memory-aware AI agents with Agentic Retrieve on Amazon Bedrock Managed Knowledge Base</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/build-memory-aware-ai-agents-with-agentic-retrieve-on-amazon-bedrock-managed-knowledge-base/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Peng Yang]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Mon, 16 Nov 2026 22:43:30 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Advanced (300)]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock Knowledge Bases]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Technical How-to]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">6d1ff2e4339ff5c12febb03d6586784822aa87ad</guid>

					<description>Amazon Bedrock Managed Knowledge Base and Amazon Bedrock AgentCore memory now store conversations, extract facts, and bring long-term memory into a single AgenticRetrieveStream call. This post shows how to build a memory-aware, multi-user assistant where each user sees only the documents they may read and each user's memory stays private.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;Users of a knowledge-grounded application expect it to remember them, not only to search well. They ask follow-up questions that make sense only in the context of the previous turn. They expect an answer about a policy or a procedure to reflect what they told the application about themselves last month. And they still expect every answer to be grounded in the documents they are allowed to read, with citations. Whether the application is a customer support assistant, a document chat inside a SaaS product, or an internal assistant over company policies, the expectation is the same.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Meeting that expectation is hard, because the context that would sharpen a search lives outside the knowledge base. A user’s location, role, or product tier was often mentioned in an unrelated conversation weeks ago. To use it, the application has to store every conversation, extract durable facts from it, decide which facts matter for the current question, and inject them into the request. When many users share the application, every one of those steps also has to be isolated per user, and so does every document.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/knowledge-bases/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock Managed Knowledge Base&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/agentcore/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore memory, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/a&gt;, now take over these steps: storing each conversation, extracting facts from it, and bringing the relevant facts into the search. You turn this on by adding a &lt;code&gt;memoryConfiguration&lt;/code&gt; to the &lt;code&gt;AgenticRetrieveStream&lt;/code&gt; request, which makes AgentCore memory a retrieval source alongside your knowledge base. Within the same call, the service restores the conversation, and the planner decides on its own when a fact about the user would help. It retrieves that fact from long-term memory and uses it to compose the sub-queries it sends to the knowledge base. After generating the answer, the service saves the turn back to memory. The same request carries a &lt;code&gt;userContext&lt;/code&gt;, so the knowledge base returns only the documents that the user’s access control lists (ACLs) allow. The integration is opt-in and backward compatible. Your application keeps only what is genuinely yours: authenticating the user and passing who they are.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This post shows how to build such an assistant for many users who share one knowledge base and one memory resource. Each user sees only the documents they may read, and each user’s memory stays private. The running example is an internal employee assistant for AnyCompany, a fictional company. Employees sign in with Amazon Cognito and ask questions over HR policies, IT guides, engineering runbooks, and sales plans. We show how to read the user’s identity from the Cognito ID token, how to pass it to &lt;code&gt;AgenticRetrieveStream&lt;/code&gt; for document ACLs and for memory, how to design memory namespaces for many users, and how to read the trace to see when the planner used memory. Finally, we make AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM) enforce per-user memory isolation even when application code gets it wrong. If you deployed the application from &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/build-multi-tenant-agentic-chat-applications-on-enterprise-data-with-amazon-bedrock-managed-knowledge-base/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Build multi-tenant agentic chat applications on enterprise data with Amazon Bedrock Managed Knowledge Base&lt;/a&gt;, this post replaces its “persist conversation history yourself” step.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="solution-overview"&gt;Solution overview&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;AnyCompany keeps its documents in Amazon Simple Storage Service (Amazon S3). One managed knowledge base syncs them through an ACL-enabled Amazon S3 data source. A global ACL file in the same bucket says which employees may read each folder: everyone reads HR and IT, only engineers read the engineering runbooks, and only salespeople read the commission plan. One AgentCore memory resource is shared by every employee. Amazon Cognito authenticates employees, and its ID token supplies two claims: the &lt;code&gt;sub&lt;/code&gt; (user ID) identifies the user’s memory, and the verified &lt;code&gt;email&lt;/code&gt; identifies the user to the knowledge base’s ACL filter. Three test users sign in: two engineers, Maya and Liam, and one salesperson, Sofia. The trace excerpts in this post are taken from a run of the sample application in the &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/sample-managed-knowledge-base-patterns/tree/main/agentcore-memory-multiuser" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;GitHub repository&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/13/ML-21969-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/13/ML-21969-1.png" alt="Architecture of the memory-aware employee assistant: application, AgenticRetrieveStream, Managed Knowledge Base, and AgentCore memory" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 1: Architecture of the memory-aware employee assistant. The application authenticates the user and constructs the request (steps 1–4). Amazon Bedrock Managed Knowledge Base restores the session, retrieves information, returns an answer, and saves the exchange to AgentCore memory (steps 5–8). Short-term events and long-term records belong to the same memory resource.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The employee signs in with Cognito and receives an ID token, a JSON Web Token (JWT) signed by the user pool.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The application’s API validates the token — an Amazon API Gateway JWT authorizer in production, a small FastAPI service in the sample. Unauthenticated requests do not reach the handler.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The handler reads two claims from the verified token: &lt;code&gt;sub&lt;/code&gt; becomes the memory &lt;code&gt;actorId&lt;/code&gt; and namespace, and &lt;code&gt;email&lt;/code&gt; becomes the &lt;code&gt;userContext.userId&lt;/code&gt;. The client body carries only &lt;code&gt;{ question, sessionId? }&lt;/code&gt;. The handler then assumes the application’s IAM role for this user, with the session tag &lt;code&gt;userId=&amp;lt;sub&amp;gt;&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The handler calls &lt;code&gt;AgenticRetrieveStream&lt;/code&gt; with the knowledge base as the retriever, &lt;code&gt;userContext&lt;/code&gt;, and a &lt;code&gt;memoryConfiguration&lt;/code&gt; bound to this user.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Managed Knowledge Base restores the conversation history from short-term memory (STM) in AgentCore memory for the actor and session identified by &lt;code&gt;sessionBinding&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Managed Knowledge Base retrieves from the knowledge base with ACL filtering based on &lt;code&gt;userContext&lt;/code&gt;. It can also retrieve the user’s configured long-term memory through speculative retrieval or memory searches selected by the planner.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Managed Knowledge Base streams a cited answer and trace events back to the handler. The handler records the traces with a correlation ID and forwards the answer and trace information to the UI.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;With &lt;code&gt;persistenceMode: DEFAULT&lt;/code&gt;, the service saves the question and generated answer to the session. AgentCore memory then extracts long-term records asynchronously according to the configured strategies.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/13/21969-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img loading="lazy" class="alignnone wp-image-144850 size-full" src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/13/21969-1.png" alt="A sequence diagram that traces a question from Your Application, through the AgenticRetrieveSystem, Managed knowledge base, and AgentCoreMemory returning responseEvent chunks." width="2440" height="1960"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;Figure 2: One &lt;code&gt;AgenticRetrieveStream&lt;/code&gt; turn with &lt;code&gt;userContext&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;memoryConfiguration&lt;/code&gt;. Everything between the first and last arrow is the service’s work.&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Managed Knowledge Base owns ingestion, ACL filtering, indexing, ranking, planning, retrieval, and generation. With &lt;code&gt;memoryConfiguration&lt;/code&gt;, it also owns session restore, memory search, and persistence. AgentCore memory owns storage, retention, encryption, and extraction. Your application owns authentication, the UI, and reading the user’s identity from the token. There is no conversation table: when the UI lists or reopens a conversation, it reads the same memory resource.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="how-identity-maps-to-the-request"&gt;How identity maps to the request&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Every value is derived by the application from the verified ID token. None comes from the request body.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Request field&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Derived from&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;What it scopes&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Enforced by&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;userContext.userId&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;ID token &lt;code&gt;email&lt;/code&gt; (verified, immutable in the pool)&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Which documents can be retrieved&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Knowledge base ACL filtering&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;memoryConfiguration.sessionBinding.actorId&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;ID token &lt;code&gt;sub&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Whose short-term memory is restored and written, and the &lt;code&gt;{actorId}&lt;/code&gt; in long-term namespaces&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;AgentCore memory keying, plus an IAM condition&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;memoryConfiguration.sessionBinding.sessionId&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Generated by the application for a new conversation. Listed with &lt;code&gt;ListSessions&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Which conversation within that actor&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Keyed under the &lt;code&gt;actorId&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;memoryConfiguration.retrievalConfigs[0].namespacePath&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;/anycompany/&amp;lt;sub&amp;gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Which long-term records the planner may search&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;IAM condition&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Two different claims do two different jobs. The knowledge base identifies users by email, because that is what the connected data source records in its ACLs. Memory needs an &lt;code&gt;actorId&lt;/code&gt; that never changes and is never reused. The Cognito &lt;code&gt;sub&lt;/code&gt; is both. An email address can be changed, or reassigned to a new employee after someone leaves, and that person would then inherit the old memory. You can use another stable user ID from your own user store as the &lt;code&gt;actorId&lt;/code&gt;. What matters is that it never moves to a different person.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="prerequisites"&gt;Prerequisites&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;This walkthrough uses a managed knowledge base and an AgentCore memory resource in the US West (Oregon) Region (us-west-2). As described in the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/kb-test-agentic-retrieve.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;agentic retrieval documentation&lt;/a&gt;, the memory resource used with this integration must be in the same AWS account as the knowledge base and in the &lt;code&gt;ACTIVE&lt;/code&gt; state.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Python 3.10 or later and Boto3 1.43.72 or later.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Permission to create an S3 bucket, a Cognito user pool, IAM roles, a knowledge base, and a memory resource. The sample’s &lt;code&gt;infra/setup.py&lt;/code&gt; creates all of these resources idempotently.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="solution-walkthrough"&gt;Solution walkthrough&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following steps create the knowledge base and its data source, set up the memory resource and Amazon Cognito, build the request, and read the trace to confirm the result.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="step-1-create-the-knowledge-base-with-an-acl-enabled-data-source"&gt;Step 1: Create the knowledge base with an ACL-enabled data source&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;A managed knowledge base needs no vector store:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Configure the service role for your setup by following &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/kb-managed-permissions.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Create a service role for managed Amazon Bedrock Knowledge Bases&lt;/a&gt;, and pass its ARN in &lt;code&gt;roleArn&lt;/code&gt;. For model access requirements, also review the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/kb-managed-prereqs.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;prerequisites for managed and custom models&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;kb = agent.create_knowledge_base(
    name="anycompany-assistant-docs",
    description="AnyCompany internal documents: HR policies, IT guides, engineering runbooks, sales plans.",
    roleArn=kb_service_role_arn,
    knowledgeBaseConfiguration={
        "type": "MANAGED",
        "managedKnowledgeBaseConfiguration": {"embeddingModelType": "MANAGED"},
    },
)["knowledgeBase"]&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Documents live in the bucket under &lt;code&gt;docs/&amp;lt;folder&amp;gt;/&lt;/code&gt;. Amazon S3 has no permission system of its own to crawl, so you supply the ACLs in a global ACL file in the same bucket. Each entry maps an S3 prefix to the emails that may read it:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-json"&gt;[
  {"keyPrefix": "s3://BUCKET/docs/hr/",
   "aclEntries": [{"Name": "maya@example.com",  "Type": "USER", "Access": "ALLOW"},
                  {"Name": "liam@example.com",  "Type": "USER", "Access": "ALLOW"},
                  {"Name": "sofia@example.com", "Type": "USER", "Access": "ALLOW"}]},
  {"keyPrefix": "s3://BUCKET/docs/engineering/",
   "aclEntries": [{"Name": "maya@example.com",  "Type": "USER", "Access": "ALLOW"},
                  {"Name": "liam@example.com",  "Type": "USER", "Access": "ALLOW"}]},
  {"keyPrefix": "s3://BUCKET/docs/sales/",
   "aclEntries": [{"Name": "sofia@example.com", "Type": "USER", "Access": "ALLOW"}]}
]&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The sample’s file also gives everyone &lt;code&gt;docs/it/&lt;/code&gt;. Then create the data source. For a managed knowledge base, every connector has the type &lt;code&gt;MANAGED_KNOWLEDGE_BASE_CONNECTOR&lt;/code&gt;, and &lt;code&gt;connectorParameters.type&lt;/code&gt; picks the source:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;ds = agent.create_data_source(
    knowledgeBaseId=kb["knowledgeBaseId"],
    name="s3-docs-acl",
    dataSourceConfiguration={
        "type": "MANAGED_KNOWLEDGE_BASE_CONNECTOR",
        "managedKnowledgeBaseConnectorConfiguration": {
            "connectorParameters": {
                "type": "S3",
                "version": "1",
                "connectionConfiguration": {"bucketName": BUCKET, "bucketOwnerAccountId": ACCOUNT_ID},
                "filterConfiguration": {"inclusionPrefixes": ["docs/"]},
                "aclEnabled": True,
                "aclConfiguration": {"globalAccessControlListS3Uri": f"s3://{BUCKET}/acl/global-acl.json"},
            }
        },
    },
)["dataSource"]&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;aclEnabled&lt;/code&gt; cannot be changed after the data source is created. &lt;code&gt;CreateDataSource&lt;/code&gt; is asynchronous, so wait for the status &lt;code&gt;AVAILABLE&lt;/code&gt;. Then call &lt;code&gt;StartIngestionJob&lt;/code&gt; and wait for &lt;code&gt;COMPLETE&lt;/code&gt;. The sync reads the documents and the ACL file and indexes each document with its ACL. In the sample, all 11 documents were scanned and indexed with 0 failures. A document that no ACL entry covers is not ingested, and a user whose email matches no entry receives no results from this data source.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The same model applies to connectors that crawl permissions from the source system, such as Microsoft SharePoint, Google Drive, and Atlassian Confluence. There, the permissions come from the source, groups are resolved from the source, and the knowledge base also checks each result against the source in real time. Your application code does not change: it still passes the user’s email in &lt;code&gt;userContext&lt;/code&gt;. For the full list, see &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/kb-managed-acl.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Access Control Lists awareness enablement&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="step-2-create-the-memory-resource-and-design-namespaces"&gt;Step 2: Create the memory resource and design namespaces&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;One memory resource serves every user. Per-user separation comes from &lt;code&gt;{actorId}&lt;/code&gt; in the namespace templates, which AgentCore memory resolves from each event at extraction time. Two choices matter.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/built-in-strategies.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Strategies&lt;/a&gt;. The two strategies extract different things. The summary strategy summarizes each conversation, so it captures facts that a user mentions in passing, such as “I’m on a Mac in the Frankfurt office.” Those facts are what personalize a later answer about, say, PTO. The user-preference strategy extracts stated preferences into structured records (“Prefers short bullet-point answers without tables”, “Always deploys to eu-central-1 first”) and does not treat incidental mentions as preferences. Summaries give you “what we discussed.” Preferences give you “how this person wants to be answered.” Use both.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Layout. &lt;code&gt;retrievalConfigs&lt;/code&gt; accepts one entry, so a single &lt;code&gt;namespacePath&lt;/code&gt; must reach everything the planner should see for a user. Put &lt;code&gt;{actorId}&lt;/code&gt; directly under the root: &lt;code&gt;namespacePath: "/anycompany/&amp;lt;sub&amp;gt;"&lt;/code&gt; then covers the profile and every conversation summary. A layout such as &lt;code&gt;/anycompany/profile/{actorId}&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;/anycompany/summaries/{actorId}&lt;/code&gt; would let one entry reach only one of them.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;memory = control.create_memory(
    name="anycompany_assistant_memory",
    eventExpiryDuration=30,          # days; raw conversation events expire after this
    # encryptionKeyArn=CMK_ARN,      # optional customer managed key
    memoryStrategies=[
        {"userPreferenceMemoryStrategy": {
            "name": "EmployeeProfile",
            "namespaces": ["/anycompany/{actorId}/profile"],
        }},
        {"summaryMemoryStrategy": {
            "name": "ConversationSummaries",
            "namespaces": ["/anycompany/{actorId}/summaries/{sessionId}"],
        }},
    ],
)["memory"]&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Wait for the memory to reach &lt;code&gt;ACTIVE&lt;/code&gt;. Creating the memory does not need a user or &lt;code&gt;actorId&lt;/code&gt;, so the order of Step 2 and Step 3 does not matter. Nothing is provisioned per user: a new Cognito user becomes a new actor the first time they ask a question.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="step-3-set-up-cognito-and-grant-the-application-permissions"&gt;Step 3: Set up Cognito and grant the application permissions&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Create a user pool and an app client without a client secret. The email decides which documents a user may read, so make it required, verified, and not changeable by the user:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;pool_id = idp.create_user_pool(
    PoolName="anycompany-assistant-employees",
    AutoVerifiedAttributes=["email"],
    Schema=[{"Name": "email", "Required": True, "Mutable": False, "AttributeDataType": "String"}],
    AdminCreateUserConfig={"AllowAdminCreateUserOnly": True},
)["UserPool"]["Id"]&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;Mutable&lt;/code&gt; cannot be changed after the pool is created. With a mutable email, a signed-in user could change their email to a colleague’s address. If your users federate from Okta or Microsoft Entra ID, map the IdP’s email to this attribute so that it matches the email in your data source’s ACLs.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The sample enables &lt;code&gt;USER_PASSWORD_AUTH&lt;/code&gt; so the local UI and tests can sign in directly. Production applications typically use Cognito managed login or AWS Amplify.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Set up an IAM role for the application. The backend assumes it once per user, with the session tag &lt;code&gt;userId=&amp;lt;sub&amp;gt;&lt;/code&gt;, and every memory statement is conditioned on that tag. This is the complete policy the sample runs with:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-json"&gt;{
  "Version": "2012-10-17",
  "Statement": [
    { "Sid": "AgenticRetrieve", "Effect": "Allow",
      "Action": "bedrock:AgenticRetrieveStream", "Resource": "*" },
    { "Sid": "KnowledgeBaseRetrieval", "Effect": "Allow",
      "Action": ["bedrock:Retrieve", "bedrock:GetDocumentContent"],
      "Resource": "arn:aws:bedrock:us-west-2:111122223333:knowledge-base/KB_ID" },
    { "Sid": "PlannerAndGeneration", "Effect": "Allow",
      "Action": "bedrock:InvokeModelWithResponseStream", "Resource": "*" },
    { "Sid": "MemoryDescribe", "Effect": "Allow",
      "Action": "bedrock-agentcore:GetMemory",
      "Resource": "arn:aws:bedrock-agentcore:us-west-2:111122223333:memory/MEMORY_ID" },
    { "Sid": "MemorySessionsScopedToCaller", "Effect": "Allow",
      "Action": ["bedrock-agentcore:ListEvents", "bedrock-agentcore:CreateEvent",
                 "bedrock-agentcore:ListSessions"],
      "Resource": "arn:aws:bedrock-agentcore:us-west-2:111122223333:memory/MEMORY_ID",
      "Condition": {"StringEquals": {"bedrock-agentcore:actorId": "${aws:PrincipalTag/userId}"}} },
    { "Sid": "MemoryRecordsScopedToCaller", "Effect": "Allow",
      "Action": ["bedrock-agentcore:RetrieveMemoryRecords", "bedrock-agentcore:ListMemoryRecords"],
      "Resource": "arn:aws:bedrock-agentcore:us-west-2:111122223333:memory/MEMORY_ID",
      "Condition": {"StringLike": {"bedrock-agentcore:namespacePath": "/anycompany/${aws:PrincipalTag/userId}/*"}} }
  ]
}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;ListEvents&lt;/code&gt; is required with &lt;code&gt;sessionBinding&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;RetrieveMemoryRecords&lt;/code&gt; with &lt;code&gt;retrievalConfigs&lt;/code&gt;, and &lt;code&gt;CreateEvent&lt;/code&gt; when &lt;code&gt;persistenceMode&lt;/code&gt; is &lt;code&gt;DEFAULT&lt;/code&gt;. &lt;code&gt;ListSessions&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;ListMemoryRecords&lt;/code&gt; are for the application’s own reads (conversation list and the “what memory knows about you” view), not for &lt;code&gt;AgenticRetrieveStream&lt;/code&gt;. The role’s trust policy allows &lt;code&gt;sts:AssumeRole&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;sts:TagSession&lt;/code&gt;, and &lt;code&gt;sts:SetSourceIdentity&lt;/code&gt; from the backend’s own identity (an AWS Lambda execution role or an Amazon Elastic Container Service (Amazon ECS) task role in production). If the memory uses a customer managed key, add &lt;code&gt;kms:Decrypt&lt;/code&gt; on that key.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="step-4-read-the-user-from-the-token-then-build-the-request"&gt;Step 4: Read the user from the token, then build the request&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Verify the ID token and read the two claims. Only the ID token carries &lt;code&gt;email&lt;/code&gt;. The access token does not:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;@dataclasses.dataclass(frozen=True)
class Principal:
    sub: str     # -&amp;gt; memory actorId and namespace
    email: str   # -&amp;gt; userContext.userId (document ACLs)

    def namespace_path(self, root: str) -&amp;gt; str:
        return f"{root}/{self.sub}"       # resolved literal, e.g. /anycompany/a8e1b390-...


def verify(id_token: str) -&amp;gt; Principal:
    key = jwks.get_signing_key_from_jwt(id_token)   # public keys from {issuer}/.well-known/jwks.json
    claims = jwt.decode(id_token, key.key, algorithms=["RS256"], issuer=ISSUER, audience=CLIENT_ID)
    if claims.get("token_use") != "id" or claims.get("email_verified") is not True:
        raise PermissionError("a verified ID token is required")
    return Principal(sub=claims["sub"], email=claims["email"].lower())&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;jwt.decode&lt;/code&gt; checks the signature, the issuer (your user pool), the expiry, and the audience (your app client). For details of the claims, see &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cognito/latest/developerguide/amazon-cognito-user-pools-using-the-id-token.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Understanding the identity (ID) token&lt;/a&gt;. With API Gateway, a JWT authorizer does the same checks and passes the claims to your handler.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Next, get the user’s AWS credentials. The backend assumes the application role for this user and caches the session until shortly before it expires:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;creds = sts.assume_role(
    RoleArn=APP_ROLE_ARN,
    RoleSessionName=f"user-{principal.sub}",
    SourceIdentity=principal.sub,
    Tags=[{"Key": "userId", "Value": principal.sub}],
)["Credentials"]&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Every call for this user, including the memory calls that agentic retrieval makes on the caller’s behalf, now runs as &lt;code&gt;assumed-role/anycompany-assistant-app-role/user-&amp;lt;sub&amp;gt;&lt;/code&gt; and carries the tag that the policy in Step 3 checks.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Decide the &lt;code&gt;sessionId&lt;/code&gt;. For a new conversation, the application generates one, such as &lt;code&gt;"conv-" + uuid4().hex&lt;/code&gt;, and returns it to the client. To continue a conversation, the client sends it back. To show a user their conversations, call &lt;code&gt;ListSessions&lt;/code&gt; with the user’s &lt;code&gt;actorId&lt;/code&gt;:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;def list_conversations(principal):
    try:
        resp = memory.list_sessions(memoryId=MEMORY_ID, actorId=principal.sub, maxResults=50)
    except memory.exceptions.ResourceNotFoundException:
        return []   # the user has not asked anything yet, so there is no actor
    return [{"sessionId": s["sessionId"], "createdAt": s["createdAt"].isoformat()} for s in resp["sessionSummaries"]]&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You do not need your own table that maps users to sessions. Accepting a &lt;code&gt;sessionId&lt;/code&gt; from the client is safe because short-term memory is keyed by &lt;code&gt;(memoryId, actorId, sessionId)&lt;/code&gt;, and the &lt;code&gt;actorId&lt;/code&gt; comes from the token. If Liam sends Maya’s &lt;code&gt;sessionId&lt;/code&gt;, it names a session inside Liam’s own space, which is empty. Agentic retrieval itself does not verify that the &lt;code&gt;actorId&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;sessionId&lt;/code&gt;, or namespace you supply belong to the user who is asking, so they must never come from the client. Keep your own session table only if you need data that memory does not hold, such as conversation titles.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Then build the request:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;def build_request(principal, question, session_id):
    return {
        # With sessionBinding, messages carries ONLY the current question.
        "messages": [{"role": "user", "content": {"text": question}}],
        "retrievers": [{"configuration": {"knowledgeBase": {"knowledgeBaseId": KB_ID}},
                        "description": KB_DESCRIPTION}],
        # Document ACLs: only documents this email may read are retrieved.
        "userContext": {"userId": principal.email},
        "agenticRetrieveConfiguration": {
            "foundationModelType": "MANAGED",
            "rerankingModelType": "MANAGED",
            "maxAgentIteration": 3,
        },
        "generateResponse": True,
        "memoryConfiguration": {
            "memoryId": MEMORY_ID,
            "sessionBinding": {"actorId": principal.sub, "sessionId": session_id},
            "persistenceMode": "DEFAULT",
            "retrievalConfigs": [{"namespacePath": principal.namespace_path("/anycompany")}],
        },
    }&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Without &lt;code&gt;userContext&lt;/code&gt;, an ACL-enabled data source returns no results at all. Compare the request with the stateless handler it replaces:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;history = history_table.query(KeyConditionExpression=Key("sessionId").eq(session_id))
messages = [*replay(history), {"role": "user", "content": {"text": question}}]
response = runtime.agentic_retrieve_stream(messages=messages, retrievers=..., agenticRetrieveConfiguration=...)
history_table.put_item(Item={"sessionId": session_id, "q": question, "a": consume(response)})&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The query before the call and the write after it are gone, and &lt;code&gt;messages&lt;/code&gt; has one element. Three rules come with &lt;code&gt;sessionBinding&lt;/code&gt;, and each is enforced with a specific &lt;code&gt;ValidationException&lt;/code&gt;. First, &lt;code&gt;messages&lt;/code&gt; carries only the current question, so you choose one history source per request. Second, &lt;code&gt;persistenceMode: DEFAULT&lt;/code&gt; (the default) writes the question and the generated answer as one event, so it requires &lt;code&gt;generateResponse: true&lt;/code&gt;. Third, &lt;code&gt;memoryId&lt;/code&gt; alone is invalid. &lt;code&gt;persistenceMode: NONE&lt;/code&gt; restores history and writes nothing, for audit or replay views.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="step-5-update-the-client-contract-and-render-history"&gt;Step 5: Update the client contract and render history&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The client sends &lt;code&gt;{ question, sessionId? }&lt;/code&gt;. On a new conversation, the application generates a &lt;code&gt;sessionId&lt;/code&gt; and returns it as the first streamed frame. The client echoes it on later turns and omits it to start over.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Because the memory resource is yours, the application reads it directly to render a conversation:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;def conversation_history(principal, session_id):
    resp = memory.list_events(memoryId=MEMORY_ID, actorId=principal.sub, sessionId=session_id,
                              includePayloads=True, maxResults=100)
    return [{"role": p["conversational"]["role"].lower(), "text": p["conversational"]["content"]["text"]}
            for ev in sorted(resp["events"], key=lambda e: e["eventTimestamp"])
            for p in ev["payload"] if "conversational" in p]&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Each persisted turn is exactly one event with two payloads: the user’s question and the final answer. The history therefore renders as a clean transcript and remains usable by other AgentCore memory clients.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="step-6-verify-from-the-trace"&gt;Step 6: Verify from the trace&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The trace tells you, per turn, what the knowledge base and memory did:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Trace event&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;When it appears&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;What to look at&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;SessionHistoryLoad&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;First event, whenever &lt;code&gt;sessionBinding&lt;/code&gt; is set&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;_memory_event_count&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;_memory_message_count&lt;/code&gt;, both 0 on a new session&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;SpeculativeRetrieval&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Before planning&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;On a new session, &lt;code&gt;retrievalMetadata&lt;/code&gt; also lists &lt;code&gt;BedrockAgentCoreMemory&lt;/code&gt;. Memory items carry &lt;code&gt;_memory_*&lt;/code&gt; metadata and no text. Knowledge base items carry &lt;code&gt;_source_uri&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;Planning&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Each iteration&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;actions[].memoryRetrieve&lt;/code&gt; means the planner chose to search memory, with a query it composed&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;Retrieval&lt;/code&gt; with &lt;code&gt;retrievalType: BedrockAgentCoreMemory&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Per planned memory action&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;_memory_record_count&lt;/code&gt;. A count of 0 is a normal result&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;A memory event with &lt;code&gt;status: FAILED&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Memory unavailable or access denied&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;failures[].message&lt;/code&gt;. Memory is best-effort: the request continues and the answer is generated from the knowledge base&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following runs show the behavior. Excerpts are verbatim, trimmed for length.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Document ACLs: Maya and Sofia, same question.&lt;/strong&gt; “What is the weekly on-call stipend?” The on-call guide is in &lt;code&gt;docs/engineering/&lt;/code&gt;. Maya’s retrieval included &lt;code&gt;docs/engineering/oncall.md&lt;/code&gt;, and the answer was “500 USD or 450 EUR per week.” Sofia’s speculative retrieval and the planner’s follow-up sub-query returned only HR and sales chunks:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-plaintext"&gt;Planning/SUCCEEDED   actions: retrieve("on-call stipend compensation policy")
Retrieval/SUCCEEDED  [BedrockKnowledgeBase]  9 items   # docs/hr/*, docs/sales/commission-plan.md&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Her answer said that no on-call stipend is documented in the sources she can access. The application made no access decision. It passed &lt;code&gt;userContext.userId: "sofia@example.com"&lt;/code&gt;, and the knowledge base did the filtering. The reverse holds for the commission plan: Sofia gets the 12% accelerator, and Maya’s retrieval does not contain &lt;code&gt;docs/sales/&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Turn 1: Maya, new conversation.&lt;/strong&gt; “How do I set up the VPN on my laptop? I’m on a Mac in the Frankfurt office.”&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-plaintext"&gt;SessionHistoryLoad/SUCCEEDED    "Restored 0 short-term memory event(s)"
SpeculativeRetrieval/SUCCEEDED  [BedrockAgentCoreMemory, BedrockKnowledgeBase]  10 items
Planning/SUCCEEDED&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The answer gives the Frankfurt gateway (&lt;code&gt;vpn-fra.anycompany.example&lt;/code&gt;) and the macOS steps. Afterwards, &lt;code&gt;ListEvents&lt;/code&gt; on the session shows one event with two payloads.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Turn 2: Maya, same conversation.&lt;/strong&gt; “It keeps disconnecting every few minutes. What should I try?” The request carried only that sentence:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-json"&gt;{"step": "SessionHistoryLoad", "status": "SUCCEEDED",
 "message": "Restored 1 short-term memory event(s)",
 "retrievalResponse": [{"metadata": {"_memory_session_id": "conv-d25dccca6cbf47a6af893621e546905d",
                                     "_memory_event_count": 1, "_memory_message_count": 2}}]}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The planner sent &lt;code&gt;retrieve("VPN disconnects every few minutes troubleshooting")&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;retrieve("Cisco Secure Client macOS frequent disconnect fix")&lt;/code&gt;. The answer resolved “it” and pointed at iCloud Private Relay, the macOS-specific cause in the guide. On a continued session, &lt;code&gt;SpeculativeRetrieval&lt;/code&gt; queries only the knowledge base. The long-term memory lookup in that step happens on new sessions.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Turn 3: Maya, a new conversation, later.&lt;/strong&gt; “How many days of PTO do I get per year, and do unused days carry over?” Her office decides the answer, and she never mentioned it in this conversation. Speculative retrieval searched long-term memory with the raw question and found one record:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-json"&gt;{"step": "SpeculativeRetrieval",
 "retrievalMetadata": [{"identifier": "JQUFTMTNZV", "retrievalType": "BedrockKnowledgeBase"},
                       {"identifier": "anycompany_assistant_memory-5llXYu8f2i", "retrievalType": "BedrockAgentCoreMemory"}],
 "retrievalResponse": [
   {"content": {"mimeType": "text/plain", "text": "# AnyCompany Paid Time Off (PTO) Policy ..."},
    "metadata": {"_document_title": "pto-policy.md", "_data_source_type": "S3",
                 "_source_uri": "https://.../docs/hr/pto-policy.md"},
    "sourceRetriever": {"identifier": "JQUFTMTNZV"}},
   {"content": {"mimeType": "text/plain"},
    "metadata": {"_memory_strategy_id": "ConversationSummaries-JFDHQVB4VU",
                 "_memory_query": "How many days of PTO do I get per year, and do unused days carry over?",
                 "_memory_namespace_path": "/anycompany/a8e1b390-10f1-70d9-ee85-b68b610ee0c4",
                 "_memory_record_count": 1},
    "sourceRetriever": {"identifier": "anycompany_assistant_memory-5llXYu8f2i"}}]}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Knowledge base and memory items share one &lt;code&gt;retrievalResponse&lt;/code&gt;. Tell them apart by &lt;code&gt;sourceRetriever.identifier&lt;/code&gt; or by the &lt;code&gt;_memory_*&lt;/code&gt; keys. The record was the summary of her VPN conversation. That was enough: the planner went straight to the answer, which said “Since your memory indicates you’re based in the Frankfurt office … you get 30 days of PTO per year, granted in full on January 1, and any unused days must be used by March 31 of the following year.” A fact from an IT conversation personalized an HR answer.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Turn 4: Liam, new conversation, identical question.&lt;/strong&gt; Same handler, same memory resource, a different token. Speculative retrieval found nothing about him, so the planner decided to search memory with its own query:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-json"&gt;{"step": "Planning", "status": "SUCCEEDED",
 "actions": [{"memoryRetrieve": {"inputQuery": {"text": "employee office location"},
                                 "memoryId": "anycompany_assistant_memory-5llXYu8f2i"}}]}
{"step": "Retrieval", "status": "SUCCEEDED",
 "message": "Retrieved 0 long-term memory record(s)",
 "retrievalMetadata": [{"identifier": "anycompany_assistant_memory-5llXYu8f2i", "retrievalType": "BedrockAgentCoreMemory"}],
 "retrievalResponse": []}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Liam received the table of all three offices and a request to say which one he is in. The application chooses where the planner may look. The planner decides whether to look and what to ask: a &lt;code&gt;memoryRetrieve&lt;/code&gt; action appears only when the planner decides that another memory search would help. Reading Maya’s &lt;code&gt;sessionId&lt;/code&gt; through the history endpoint with Liam’s token returned an empty list.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table style="border: 0;border-collapse: collapse;margin: 0 auto;width: 100%"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td style="border: 0;padding: 0 4px;text-align: center;width: 50%"&gt;&lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/13/ML-21969-2.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/13/ML-21969-2.png" alt="Maya’s assistant answer with a memory hit, giving a Frankfurt-specific VPN response" width="390"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td style="border: 0;padding: 0 4px;text-align: center;width: 50%"&gt;&lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/13/ML-21969-3.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/13/ML-21969-3.png" alt="Liam’s assistant answer with zero memory records, showing the generic three-office table" width="390"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;Figure 3: The same question from two users. Maya’s trace shows a memory hit and a Frankfurt-specific answer. Liam’s trace shows zero memory records and the generic table.&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Long-term extraction is asynchronous and runs after the turn is persisted. In our run, the first record appeared 81 seconds after turn 2, but the timing is not a commitment. Design for eventual availability. A fact stated in this conversation is not guaranteed to be retrievable in the next one immediately. The sample polls &lt;code&gt;ListMemoryRecords&lt;/code&gt; before running turn 3.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="security-and-multi-user-isolation"&gt;Security and multi-user isolation&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;In this walkthrough, the application authenticates. The knowledge base filters.&lt;/strong&gt; ACL-aware retrieval is filtering, not authorization. Take the email only from a verified ID token with &lt;code&gt;email_verified: true&lt;/code&gt;, make it immutable in the pool, and handle email reuse: if an address is reassigned to a different person, that person gets the old owner’s document access. The knowledge base fails closed: a document with no ACL, or an ACL error, means fewer results, not extra ones. If results are missing, check that the email matches the ACL entry.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Identity comes from the token&lt;/strong&gt; (Step 4) for memory too: &lt;code&gt;actorId&lt;/code&gt; and namespace come from the verified &lt;code&gt;sub&lt;/code&gt;, never from the client.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;IAM condition keys, as defense in depth.&lt;/strong&gt; AgentCore memory exposes &lt;code&gt;bedrock-agentcore:actorId&lt;/code&gt; on &lt;code&gt;ListEvents&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;CreateEvent&lt;/code&gt;, and &lt;code&gt;ListSessions&lt;/code&gt;, and &lt;code&gt;bedrock-agentcore:namespacePath&lt;/code&gt; on &lt;code&gt;RetrieveMemoryRecords&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;ListMemoryRecords&lt;/code&gt;. The memory calls that agentic retrieval makes during a request are authorized against the calling identity. That is why the caller’s policy must grant those actions, and why the conditions in Step 3 apply to them. With the session assumed for Maya, a request bound to Maya’s &lt;code&gt;actorId&lt;/code&gt; proceeds normally. A request that binds Liam’s &lt;code&gt;actorId&lt;/code&gt; is the bug that this policy guards against, and it is denied at every memory operation:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-plaintext"&gt;SessionHistoryLoad/FAILED      failures: ["AccessDeniedException"]
SpeculativeRetrieval/SUCCEEDED failures: ["AccessDeniedException"]   # memory leg denied, KB leg answered&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Nothing was written to Liam’s memory: his session count stayed at 0. The answer was still generated from the knowledge base. Because each user has their own role session, AWS also sees which user each call is for: the caller is &lt;code&gt;assumed-role/anycompany-assistant-app-role/user-&amp;lt;sub&amp;gt;&lt;/code&gt; with &lt;code&gt;SourceIdentity=&amp;lt;sub&amp;gt;&lt;/code&gt;, which the session holder cannot change.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Two further points. The memory is a customer-owned resource that other systems in your account may also write to. Treat its contents as input, and attach an Amazon Bedrock guardrail to the request through &lt;code&gt;policyConfiguration&lt;/code&gt;. And plan the data lifecycle. Raw events expire per &lt;code&gt;eventExpiryDuration&lt;/code&gt;, but extracted records do not. A user-deletion workflow therefore covers &lt;code&gt;DeleteEvent&lt;/code&gt; for the actor’s sessions and &lt;code&gt;DeleteMemoryRecord&lt;/code&gt; under the actor’s namespace.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="observability-and-failure-behavior"&gt;Observability and failure behavior&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Log the full trace with a correlation ID per turn. The planner’s sub-queries are as worth auditing as its final results. &lt;code&gt;AWS/Bedrock/KnowledgeBases&lt;/code&gt; reports &lt;code&gt;Invocations&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;ClientErrors&lt;/code&gt;, and &lt;code&gt;TotalIterationCount&lt;/code&gt; for &lt;code&gt;Operation=AgenticRetrieveStream&lt;/code&gt;. &lt;code&gt;AWS/Bedrock-AgentCore&lt;/code&gt; reports &lt;code&gt;Invocations&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;Latency&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;Errors&lt;/code&gt;, and &lt;code&gt;UserErrors&lt;/code&gt; per memory operation, and &lt;code&gt;InputTokenUsage&lt;/code&gt;/&lt;code&gt;OutputTokenUsage&lt;/code&gt; per strategy for extraction. With Amazon CloudWatch Transaction Search enabled, AgentCore Observability, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, shows a turn’s spans end to end.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Memory operations are best-effort by design. If a memory call fails or is denied, the request does not fail. The trace carries a &lt;code&gt;FAILED&lt;/code&gt; memory event with the failure message, and the turn continues without that memory. The answer is generated from the knowledge base. Decide whether your UI surfaces that degraded state.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="considerations"&gt;Considerations&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Use a stable &lt;code&gt;sessionId&lt;/code&gt; for the life of a conversation. The API accepts a fresh ID per request, but a fresh ID silently breaks continuity and produces one summary record per turn.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Use &lt;code&gt;namespacePath&lt;/code&gt; to reach all of a user’s long-term records. &lt;code&gt;retrievalConfigs&lt;/code&gt; accepts one entry with up to five &lt;code&gt;metadataFilters&lt;/code&gt;. &lt;code&gt;namespace&lt;/code&gt; matches by prefix and &lt;code&gt;namespacePath&lt;/code&gt; matches by hierarchy, so &lt;code&gt;namespacePath&lt;/code&gt; fits per-user IDs under the root. Supply namespaces fully resolved, because a template such as &lt;code&gt;/anycompany/{actorId}&lt;/code&gt; is rejected.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Expect only &lt;code&gt;USER&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;ASSISTANT&lt;/code&gt; conversational events to be restored. Events with the &lt;code&gt;TOOL&lt;/code&gt; or &lt;code&gt;OTHER&lt;/code&gt; role that other systems write are skipped.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Keep sending &lt;code&gt;messages&lt;/code&gt; instead of &lt;code&gt;sessionBinding&lt;/code&gt; when your application needs to control exactly which history each request carries. For example, your existing history store may be the system of record for conversations, or you may edit or redact history before sending it. With &lt;code&gt;sessionBinding&lt;/code&gt;, the service restores and persists the conversation for you. You can’t combine the two in one request.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;A request can carry up to five knowledge bases. With more than one retriever, the planner routes each sub-query based on the knowledge base &lt;code&gt;description&lt;/code&gt;, so write it carefully.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Plan for latency and cost. The session restore adds on the order of 200 milliseconds. In our runs, a full turn with one knowledge base, ACLs, and memory took 4–10 seconds with the managed model. Planning iterations drive that time, not memory. These are observed figures, not service commitments. Agentic retrieval is priced per call plus underlying retrievals, and the memory operations made during the call are billed as AgentCore memory usage. For details, see &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/pricing/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock pricing&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/agentcore/pricing/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore pricing&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="clean-up"&gt;Clean up&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Run &lt;code&gt;python infra/teardown.py&lt;/code&gt; in the sample to delete the knowledge base, the S3 bucket and its objects, the memory resource (including conversation history and long-term records), the Cognito user pool, and the IAM roles.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="conclusion"&gt;Conclusion&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Amazon Bedrock Managed Knowledge Base already gave you the retrieval stack as a service. That stack covers ingestion, document ACLs, indexing, ranking, and the planning loop in &lt;code&gt;AgenticRetrieveStream&lt;/code&gt; that turns a hard question into a cited answer. With &lt;code&gt;memoryConfiguration&lt;/code&gt;, the same call now carries the conversation and the user’s history. For a multi-user application, that removed the conversation store, the history replay, and the extraction pipeline. What remained was a short list of responsibilities. Authenticate the user and read two claims from the ID token: pass the verified email as &lt;code&gt;userContext&lt;/code&gt; so that the knowledge base filters documents, and use the &lt;code&gt;sub&lt;/code&gt; as the &lt;code&gt;actorId&lt;/code&gt; for one memory resource with &lt;code&gt;{actorId}&lt;/code&gt;-rooted namespaces. Use IAM condition keys on a per-user role session as a second line of defense that holds through the agentic path.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To get started, deploy the sample from the &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/sample-managed-knowledge-base-patterns/tree/main/agentcore-memory-multiuser" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;GitHub repository&lt;/a&gt;. Then read the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/APIReference/API_agent-runtime_AgenticRetrieveStream.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AgenticRetrieveStream API reference&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/kb-managed-acl.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Access Control Lists awareness enablement&lt;/a&gt;, and &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/kb-test-agentic-retrieve.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Use AgentCore memory with agentic retrieval&lt;/a&gt;. For the retrieval loop itself, see &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/agentic-retrieval-for-amazon-bedrock-managed-knowledge-base/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Agentic retrieval for Amazon Bedrock Managed Knowledge Base&lt;/a&gt;. For memory concepts, see &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/amazon-bedrock-agentcore-memory-building-context-aware-agents/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore memory: Building context-aware agents&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/p&gt;
