			</item>
		<item>
		<title>How MoEngage built agentic customer engagement on Amazon Bedrock AgentCore</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/how-moengage-built-agentic-customer-engagement-on-amazon-bedrock-agentcore/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Ashwin Srinivasan]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Mon, 16 Nov 2026 18:16:07 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock AgentCore]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Customer Solutions]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">2915fd77bac534686e62cc7588876a9c0f53a15a</guid>

					<description>MoEngage built Merlin AI, a set of AI agents on Amazon Bedrock and Amazon Bedrock AgentCore that let marketing and product teams draft, analyze, and adjust campaigns from a single conversation. Learn how MoEngage turned its existing APIs into MCP tools and kept each tenant's data isolated while giving agents secure access to live platform data.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;This post was co-written with Ashwin Srinivasan, Nishan Das and Karthik Baile from MoEngage.&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;A marketing or product team member planning a weekend flash sale often opens three tools before writing a single line of copy. First they open the analytics dashboard to see what worked last time, then the content library to find the right tone, and then the campaign builder to build the campaign itself. Repeat that across email, push, SMS, and WhatsApp, and much of a marketing team’s week goes to navigating tools instead of making decisions.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The MoEngage Model Context Protocol (MCP) Connector gives an MCP-compatible assistant, such as Claude or ChatGPT, secure, tenant-scoped access to a brand’s segments, campaign history, and content library.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This capability points to a broader shift in software platforms. Beyond a user interface for business users and an API for engineering teams, a third layer is emerging, where a user describes an outcome in plain language and an agent carries out the steps across the platform. For a customer engagement platform, that means a capability already exposed as an API can become a tool an agent calls, and a campaign that once moved across several tools can move through one conversation instead.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;MoEngage, the agentic customer engagement platform that more than 1,350 consumer brands use across channels including push, email, in-app, SMS, WhatsApp, and Rich Communication Services (RCS), set out to build this layer. MoEngage built Merlin AI, a set of artificial intelligence (AI) agents that work with live platform data. Marketing and product teams can now draft, analyze, and adjust campaigns from a single conversation, while agents decide what to send each customer. One customer, Glance, saw a 38% uplift in push notification click-through rates as a result.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;MoEngage treated this as an architecture problem. The question was how to give agents secure, tenant-scoped access to live data without rebuilding hundreds of existing APIs. In this post, we walk through how MoEngage solved it with Amazon Bedrock and Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, a platform to build, connect, and optimize AI agents at scale with any framework or model. The post covers the four agent modes in Merlin AI, how MoEngage exposed its existing APIs as MCP tools, and how the architecture keeps each tenant’s data isolated. It closes with the results MoEngage customers have seen and the pattern other software providers can reuse.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="solution-overview-four-agent-modes-in-merlin-ai"&gt;Solution overview: Four agent modes in Merlin AI&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Merlin AI runs four kinds of agents. All four share the same foundation: foundation models in Amazon Bedrock for reasoning and generation, AgentCore runtime, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, for hosting, and MCP for standardized access to data. Across all four modes, Merlin AI uses Anthropic Claude Sonnet 4.6, Claude Sonnet 5, and Claude Sonnet 5.5 on Amazon Bedrock for reasoning, tool-call planning, and content generation. For the full list of models, see &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/models-supported.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Supported foundation models in Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="embedded-agents-in-merlin-ai-studio"&gt;Embedded agents in Merlin AI Studio&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Merlin AI Studio places purpose-built agents at the exact point in the workflow where marketing and product teams do that piece of work:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Copywriter&lt;/strong&gt; generates personalized push and transactional copy that stays consistent with the brand voice.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Designer&lt;/strong&gt; produces a marketing banner or image from a text description.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Segment Assist&lt;/strong&gt; turns a plain-language audience description into the correct set of attributes and filters. For example, a marketing or product team member can ask for users who abandoned a cart in the last 2 hours.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Jinja Assist&lt;/strong&gt; converts natural-language requests into working Jinja and Liquid markup for dynamic content.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;In-app Template Builder&lt;/strong&gt; creates responsive, accessible HTML templates for in-app messages, including gamified formats, lead-capture pop-ups, and surveys.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Flow Assist&lt;/strong&gt; turns a high-level idea into a ready-to-deploy customer journey. Behind the scenes, a sequence of specialized steps interprets the request, proposes paths, builds the flow structure, refines it with historical data, and packages it for deployment.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="conversational-agents"&gt;Conversational agents&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Conversational agents work across campaigns, analytics, segments, and flows. A user can ask a question in plain language and the agent calls the right tools to answer it or act on it. The Campaign Insights Agent, embedded on the analytics page, delegates to specialized sub-agents. One sub-agent runs analysis across several metrics at once. A second diagnoses why a metric is underperforming, and a third compares performance across time periods or segments. A final step formats the findings into a clear answer.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="custom-agents"&gt;Custom agents&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Custom agents let lifecycle and customer relationship management (CRM) teams design agents that run continuously on MoEngage data and tools, inside rules the product and marketing team defines. Every agent shows its work through an activity log of the data it pulled, the decisions it made, the channels it touched, and the content it sent. Teams can run an agent with full autonomy or require human review before anything executes.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="decisioning-agents"&gt;Decisioning agents&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Decisioning agents act for each individual customer. Each agent decides what to say, when, how often, and on which channel, and it learns from every outcome. Product and marketing teams set the content, goals, and guardrails, and the agents handle the rest, with visibility into every decision. Offer Decisioning selects the best offer on web and app surfaces. Campaign Decisioning delivers 1:1 decisions across email, push, SMS, and WhatsApp. Together with workflow agents, this puts both halves of agentic marketing in one system.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="architecture-overview"&gt;Architecture overview&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The architecture has four layers. Each layer has a single responsibility and can change without affecting the others.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Clients.&lt;/strong&gt; Merlin AI agents inside the MoEngage product, and external MCP clients such as Claude and ChatGPT, through the MoEngage MCP Connector, connect to the MoEngage MCP server. Clients that support MCP can connect.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;MCP server on AgentCore runtime.&lt;/strong&gt; MoEngage hosts its MCP server on AgentCore runtime. The server exposes MoEngage platform capabilities as MCP tools. It does not run its own model. Reasoning happens in the agent that calls it.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;MoEngage platform APIs.&lt;/strong&gt; The MCP server calls the same OpenAPI 3.0-compatible APIs that MoEngage customers already use. These APIs enforce existing permissions and data residency rules.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Foundation models in Amazon Bedrock.&lt;/strong&gt; Agents use foundation models in Amazon Bedrock to plan tool calls, generate content, and summarize results. For model availability by Region, see &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/models-regions.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Supported models by AWS Region in Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In a typical request, a user signs in to MoEngage and asks a question. The client sends the request with the user’s existing JSON Web Token (JWT). AgentCore runtime validates the token and starts an isolated session for that user. The agent asks a model in Amazon Bedrock which tools to call. The MCP server calls the MoEngage APIs with the user’s tenant context and returns the results. The agent then composes the answer.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following diagram shows how these components connect.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/06/ML-21091-1-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/06/ML-21091-1-1.png" alt="Four-layer Merlin AI architecture: clients, the MCP server on AgentCore runtime, MoEngage platform APIs, and foundation models in Amazon Bedrock" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 1: MoEngage Merlin AI architecture on Amazon Bedrock and AgentCore runtime.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="turning-existing-apis-into-mcp-tools"&gt;Turning existing APIs into MCP tools&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;MoEngage opens its platform to agents through an MCP server and agent-callable APIs. Its platform APIs for campaigns, analytics, segments, content, and flows were already OpenAPI 3.0-compatible, so MoEngage used the FastMCP framework to generate MCP tools from those specifications. Each operation becomes a tool with a structured schema, a description, and parameter validation.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following simplified example shows the pattern:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;import httpx
from fastmcp import FastMCP

spec = httpx.get(MOENGAGE_OPENAPI_URL).json()
client = httpx.AsyncClient(base_url=MOENGAGE_API_BASE)
mcp = FastMCP.from_openapi(openapi_spec=spec, client=client, name='moengage')

if __name__ == '__main__':
    mcp.run(transport='streamable-http', host='0.0.0.0', port=8000)&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The tools fall into five groups:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Campaign tools&lt;/strong&gt; create, edit, schedule, and send campaigns across channels.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Analytics tools&lt;/strong&gt; query delivery funnels, click-through rates, conversions, and cohort performance.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Segmentation tools&lt;/strong&gt; list, inspect, and create segments from behavioral and attribute filters.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Content tools&lt;/strong&gt; manage content blocks, templates, and dynamic content rules.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Flow tools&lt;/strong&gt; inspect, pause, or modify automation journeys.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The tools come from the specifications, so they stay in sync with the APIs. When a team updates a specification, the tool definitions update with it.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="keeping-tenants-isolated-and-identity-out-of-the-models-reach"&gt;Keeping tenants isolated and identity out of the model’s reach&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;A multi-tenant system cannot let a model decide whose data it is reading. MoEngage made three design choices to help prevent that. First, the MCP server reuses the existing authentication model, accepting the same JWT-based authentication that MoEngage APIs already use, and AgentCore runtime validates each token before a request reaches the server. Second, workspace and user context travel in request headers that AgentCore runtime forwards to the server rather than as tool arguments, so the model does not see these values and cannot change them. Third, AgentCore runtime runs each user session in its own isolated environment, so one user’s conversation cannot read another’s session state.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;On top of this, Amazon Bedrock Guardrails apply content safety and personally identifiable information (PII) filters to agent responses. A backend-for-frontend layer then validates the structure of generated content before the MoEngage interface renders it.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Give users control over agents.&lt;/strong&gt; Users set guardrails on what each agent can do, review every action in its activity log, and choose between autonomous and human-reviewed execution.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="why-moengage-chose-agentcore-runtime"&gt;Why MoEngage chose AgentCore runtime&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;AgentCore runtime provides the production capabilities that a multi-tenant MCP server needs, and each one addresses a specific requirement of MoEngage’s workload. AgentCore runtime scales sessions as demand changes, so MoEngage does not plan capacity for traffic bursts across its customer workspaces. Each session runs in its own isolated environment by default, which keeps one brand’s conversation and data separate from another’s. The built-in JWT authorizer validates tokens against MoEngage’s existing identity provider, so the MCP server uses the same authentication model as the APIs.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Hosting on AgentCore runtime is also framework-agnostic. MoEngage can change models or agent frameworks without touching the MCP server, which matters as the agent layer evolves. Built-in metrics, logs, and traces through Amazon CloudWatch give MoEngage engineers visibility into every session. Together, these capabilities let the team focus on agent logic and marketing intelligence rather than hosting infrastructure.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Inference through Amazon Bedrock gave MoEngage two more benefits. Merlin AI agents run on Anthropic Claude Sonnet 4.6, Claude Sonnet 5, and Claude Sonnet 5.5 on Amazon Bedrock, and MoEngage moves between these versions through the same Amazon Bedrock API. MoEngage also pays per token instead of operating GPU infrastructure. Amazon Bedrock does not use customer prompts or responses to train foundation models, which matters for a system that processes customer engagement data at scale.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="one-campaign-one-conversation"&gt;One campaign, one conversation&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following example shows how a user builds a campaign without leaving the conversation.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Ask for the draft. The user types:&lt;br&gt; 
  &lt;blockquote&gt;
   &lt;p&gt;Draft a weekend flash sale push for our high-value segment, using the same tone as our best-performing December campaign.&lt;/p&gt;
  &lt;/blockquote&gt; &lt;p&gt;The agent calls the analytics tools to find the top December campaign, the segmentation tools to confirm the high-value segment, and the content tools to pull the brand voice. It returns a draft with several copy variants.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Check what underperformed. The user asks:&lt;br&gt; 
  &lt;blockquote&gt;
   &lt;p&gt;Which push notifications last month had below 2% click-through rate (CTR), and what do they have in common?&lt;/p&gt;
  &lt;/blockquote&gt; &lt;p&gt;The Campaign Insights Agent compares those campaigns and reports the shared pattern, such as send time or audience overlap.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Adjust the journey. The user asks:&lt;br&gt; 
  &lt;blockquote&gt;
   &lt;p&gt;Show me the drop-off at step 3 of our onboarding flow, and pause the SMS branch if it’s below 5% conversion.&lt;/p&gt;
  &lt;/blockquote&gt; &lt;p&gt;The agent reads the flow state through the flow tools and pauses the branch after the user confirms. This is the human-review mode in practice.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Each step uses the same tools that power the MoEngage interface. The user does not open a separate dashboard.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="results"&gt;Results&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Merlin AI agents on this architecture have been in production for more than 6 months. One customer, Glance, reached campaign go-live 50% faster and saw a 38% uplift in push notification click-through rates. With Merlin AI Copywriter, Glance teams also produce about three times more content variants per campaign.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Across the platform, MoEngage reports a 60–70% reduction in manual effort by marketing and product teams for channel, timing, and frequency decisions, and a 20–30% improvement in conversion rates from goal-based decisioning. Managed, pay-per-token inference in Amazon Bedrock reduced AI infrastructure costs by more than 50% compared with self-hosted models.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;AgentCore feature availability varies by AWS Region. For supported Regions, see the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore documentation&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="conclusion"&gt;Conclusion&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;MoEngage made its platform agent-ready without rebuilding it. Existing OpenAPI specifications became MCP tools. AgentCore runtime provided hosting, authentication, and session isolation. Foundation models in Amazon Bedrock provided reasoning and generation. The result is a platform where marketing and product teams move from question to action in one conversation, where MoEngage engineers spend their time on marketing intelligence rather than infrastructure, and where agents handle workflows for the team and decisions for every customer while users stay in control.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The same pattern applies to other software providers with a mature API surface. Start from your existing specifications, keep identity outside the model, and let a managed runtime handle the production concerns.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To build your own MCP server on the same foundation, start with the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore documentation&lt;/a&gt; and follow the AgentCore runtime guide to host it. To choose a foundation model for your agents and review the security and data-handling controls, see the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/what-is-bedrock.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock User Guide&lt;/a&gt;. To generate MCP tools from your existing OpenAPI specifications, review the &lt;a href="https://gofastmcp.com/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;FastMCP documentation&lt;/a&gt;. To see Merlin AI in action, visit &lt;a href="https://www.moengage.com/merlin-ai/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;MoEngage Merlin AI&lt;/a&gt;. To plan a similar architecture, contact your AWS account team or Solutions Architect.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/p&gt;

			</item>
		<item>
		<title>Modernizing mainframe Assembler to Java using Amazon Bedrock and AWS Transform</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/modernizing-mainframe-assembler-to-java-using-amazon-bedrock-and-aws-transform/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Abhishek Ashish]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Mon, 16 Nov 2026 17:29:29 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[AWS Transform]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Best Practices]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Intermediate (200)]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">8d03145658ec1cdef2b0aca5628c08dfaffefb5a</guid>

					<description>Legacy mainframe Assembler applications are costly to maintain and hard to staff. This post presents a multi-stage, AI-powered methodology using Amazon Bedrock, Kiro IDE, and AWS Transform to extract business rules, convert Assembler through an intermediate COBOL stage, and deploy modernized Java Spring Boot applications on AWS.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;Imagine a batch job that has run every night for decades without failing, and the only person who understands its code is about to retire. We have seen this situation again and again while working with banking, insurance, and airline customers. Their Assembler programs still run critical business operations, but fewer people can read them each year. Rewriting them by hand takes years, risks breaking business rules nobody remembers writing, and still depends on the same experts who are leaving. &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/transform/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Transform&lt;/a&gt; change that equation.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This post shows you a multi-stage transformation methodology using &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href="https://kiro.dev/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Kiro IDE&lt;/a&gt; (an AI-powered integrated development environment), and &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/transform/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Transform&lt;/a&gt; that can help reduce modernization time compared to manual approaches while maintaining business logic integrity through validation checkpoints. You will learn how to systematically extract business rules from Assembler code, convert them through an intermediate COBOL stage, and deploy modernized applications as Java Spring Boot services on AWS.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="prerequisites"&gt;Prerequisites&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Before starting the modernization process, make sure you have:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Active &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/account/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS account&lt;/a&gt; with least-privilege AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM) permissions for Amazon Bedrock, AWS Transform, and Amazon Simple Storage Service (Amazon S3).&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Access to &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/q/developer/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Q Developer&lt;/a&gt; or &lt;a href="https://kiro.dev/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Kiro&lt;/a&gt; for AI-assisted code analysis.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Access to AWS Transform for COBOL to Java conversion.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Access to Amazon Bedrock with appropriate foundation models (FMs).&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;An &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/s3/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon S3&lt;/a&gt; bucket configured for source code and artifact storage, with encryption and Block Public Access enabled.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Knowledge of the target business domain and application functionality.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Availability of business and technical subject matter experts (SMEs) for validation sessions.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Source Assembler code inventory with available documentation.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Test data sets with expected outputs for functional equivalence testing.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="solution-overview"&gt;Solution overview&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You can transform legacy mainframe Assembler applications tightly bounded with COBOL into Java Spring Boot applications built for the AWS cloud through a systematic five-phase approach. This multi-stage methodology, converting Assembler to COBOL as an intermediate step before Java transformation, reduces risk and preserves business logic with greater reliability than direct Assembler-to-Java conversion. The intermediate COBOL stage provides a structured, human-readable format that subject matter experts (SMEs) can validate before final transformation. This makes sure that business rules remain intact throughout the modernization journey. The following sections describe both the current mainframe architecture and the target cloud-focused state.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="current-state"&gt;Current state&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Your current architecture represents a traditional mainframe environment where Assembler programs execute batch processes, connected with COBOL, interact with databases, and process files. At its core, JCL (Job Control Language) jobs trigger Assembler program execution at scheduled intervals. These programs read input from VSAM (Virtual Storage Access Method) and sequential files and perform operations against IBM Db2 for z/OS. Your business logic executes using low-level register operations (direct CPU register manipulation) and memory management. Output is written back to files or database tables, and job completion status is reported through JCL return codes. All of this runs on IBM z/OS mainframe infrastructure.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 782px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/10/ML-20283-1-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/10/ML-20283-1-1.png" alt="Mainframe architecture with JCL-triggered Assembler batch programs accessing VSAM files and IBM Db2 for z/OS" width="772"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 1: Mainframe Assembler architecture&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="target-state"&gt;Target state&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The modernization solution helps you transform legacy mainframe Assembler applications into Java applications built for the AWS cloud through a multi-phase, AI-powered workflow. The architecture uses Amazon Bedrock and Kiro IDE for AI-assisted code analysis and business rule extraction, and AWS Transform for automated COBOL-to-Java conversion.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;If COBOL source code is already available, you follow the conventional approach and provide it directly to AWS Transform for conversion to Java Spring Boot applications.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;With this solution, you can achieve:&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Reduced transformation time compared to manual approaches.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Systematic business logic preservation through validation checkpoints.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Human-in-the-loop validation at critical stages.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Automated code conversion with functional equivalence testing.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/10/ML-20283-2-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/10/ML-20283-2-1.png" alt="End-to-end AWS workflow transforming Assembler to Java through five phases, from business rules extraction to COBOL-to-Java" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 2: End-to-end Assembler-to-Java transformation architecture on AWS, showing five main phases: business rules extraction, spec-driven data flow, Assembler-to-COBOL conversion, validation, and COBOL-to-Java transformation&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You begin the transformation workflow by analyzing Assembler code to generate Business Rules Extraction (BRE) documentation. You then create detailed functional specifications with human-in-the-loop refinement. The validated specifications guide the Assembler-to-COBOL conversion, generating intermediate COBOL code that bridges the gap between low-level hardware instructions and business-oriented constructs. This COBOL code undergoes functional equivalence testing before AWS Transform converts it to Java, with validation checkpoints at each phase to reduce risk and verify business continuity.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="business-rules-extraction-with-ai"&gt;Business rules extraction with AI&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The quality of your Business Rules Extraction (BRE) directly impacts transformation accuracy, so focus carefully on this phase. Use Amazon Bedrock, through Kiro IDE or the Amazon Bedrock console, along with AWS Transform for mainframe to extract and document business rules from your Assembler source code.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You can use the prompts in this post directly in Amazon Bedrock through the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/playgrounds.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Chat Playground&lt;/a&gt;, or within Kiro IDE using its built-in &lt;a href="https://kiro.dev/docs" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AI chat and Spec panel&lt;/a&gt;. Replace the placeholder values with your actual Assembler code and context before running.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Amazon Bedrock is the inference platform behind the first four phases: business rules extraction, specification creation, Assembler-to-COBOL conversion, and validation. Choose a foundation model with strong code reasoning and a large context window, because Assembler programs are long and dense. Set the temperature to 0 so you get consistent output across runs. To process many programs, call the same prompts through the Amazon Bedrock Converse API and store each output in your Amazon S3 bucket so it can feed the next phase. Amazon Bedrock doesn’t use your prompts or model outputs to train AWS models or share them with third parties. For details, see &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/data-protection.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Data protection in Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt;. Use sanitized test data instead of production data or personally identifiable information (PII).&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The more context you provide to the AI, the better it understands the business intent behind the code. Include relevant documentation, data dictionaries, and available business context alongside the Assembler code when prompting.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Note:&lt;/strong&gt; Save the output of each prompt as you go. You will need them as inputs in the next phase.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Example prompt for context-aware analysis:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-prompt"&gt;Extract the business rules and data mappings from this Assembly code.
[PASTE YOUR ASSEMBLY CODE HERE]

## EXTRACT

1.Business Rules: Identify EVERY rule including conditional logic, calculations, validations, transformations, error handling, and loops.

For each rule provide:
- Unique ID (BR001, BR002, etc.)
- Name and description
- Business purpose
- Input fields/registers
- Output fields/registers
- Logic in plain English
- Conditions that trigger the rule

2.Data Mappings: Identify the data transformations with:
- Source field (register or memory location)
- Target field (COBOL variable name)
- Transformation logic
- Validation rules

## RETURN JSON
{  
"program_name": "string",
  "business_rules": [
    {
      "id": "BR001",
      "name": "string",
      "description": "string",
      "purpose": "string",
      "inputs": ["array"],
      "outputs": ["array"],
      "logic": "string",
      "conditions": ["array"]
    }
  ],
  "data_mappings": [
    {
      "source_field": "string",
      "target_field": "string",
      "transformation": "string",
      "validation_rules": ["array"]
    }
  ],
  "summary": "string"
}
&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You can customize this prompt by adding domain-specific terminology from your business context. When you run this prompt, you will see structured JSON output that you can use for automated validation and direct integration with downstream transformation tools. Data mapping accuracy directly affects conversion quality. Incomplete mappings can lead to lost or corrupted business logic.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="validate-with-smes"&gt;Validate with SMEs&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Treat AI-generated BRE documents as drafts, not final outputs. Have subject matter experts review and validate the extracted rules before moving forward. Document discrepancies in a validation log and update the BRE based on SME feedback before proceeding to specification creation.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To help SMEs validate the rules, supplement textual BRE documents with visual aids like flowcharts and data flow diagrams. Maintain a validation checklist covering business rule accuracy, data mapping correctness, edge case handling, and error condition completeness.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="extracting-spec-driven-data-flow"&gt;Extracting spec-driven data flow&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;After&amp;nbsp;you validate business rules with SMEs, translate them into detailed functional specifications that will guide code conversion. This spec-driven approach confirms that business requirements are clearly documented and agreed upon before code transformation begins, reducing costly rework later.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Use the extracted BRE documents to create complete functional specifications that guide COBOL code generation. These specifications should include program overview, COBOL data structures, process flow design, and business rules coverage mapping.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Example prompt for functional specification generation:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-prompt"&gt;Generate a complete functional specification for COBOL code generation based on the following Business Rules Extraction (BRE).

[PASTE THE BRE JSON HERE]

Create a comprehensive specification including:

## 1. PROGRAM OVERVIEW
- Program purpose and key functionality
- Business rules coverage summary

## 2. COBOL DATA STRUCTURES
Design data structures with:
- COBOL naming: UPPERCASE-WITH-HYPHENS
- Appropriate PIC clauses:
  * PIC 9(n) / S9(n) for integers (S for signed)
  * PIC 9(n)V9(m) / S9(n)V9(m) for decimals
  * PIC X(n) for alphanumeric, PIC A(n) for alphabetic
  * COMP-3 for packed decimal (efficient storage)
- Hierarchical levels (01, 05, 10, 15)
- 88-level condition names for flags

Include:
- The fields from business rules (inputs/outputs)
- The fields from data mappings (source/target)
- Additional fields for calculations, flags, error handling

## 3. PROCESS FLOW DESIGN
Create step-by-step process flow with:
- Sequential procedure steps (initialization → processing → output)
- Decision points and validation steps
- Calculation sequences and error handling

For each step specify:
- Step number and name
- Description
- Business rules implemented (BR IDs)
- Input/output data

## 4. BUSINESS RULES COVERAGE MAP
Map each business rule to:
- Implementation location (procedure/paragraph name)
- Data structures used
- Dependencies on other rules

Return as JSON:
{
  "program_name": "string",
  "overview": "string",
  "data_structures": [
    {
      "name": "WORKING-STORAGE-SECTION",
      "fields": [
        {
          "level": "01",
          "name": "FIELD-NAME",
          "type": "PIC X(10)",
          "description": "string"
        }
      ]
    }
  ],
  "process_flows": [
    {
      "step": 1,
      "name": "PROCEDURE-NAME",
      "description": "string",
      "business_rules": ["BR001"],
      "inputs": ["array"],
      "outputs": ["array"]
    }
  ],
  "business_rules_coverage": {
    "BR001": "Implementation details"
  }
}
&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You can adjust the data structure specifications based on your COBOL standards. When you run this prompt, you will receive a complete functional specification with full traceability from business rules to COBOL structures, ready for SME review and direct integration with Kiro for code generation.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="converting-legacy-assembler-to-cobol"&gt;Converting legacy Assembler to COBOL&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;With validated functional specifications in hand, the next phase uses these structured outputs to guide the Assembler-to-COBOL conversion. The intermediate COBOL step helps you bridge the gap between low-level hardware instructions and business-oriented constructs, which helps you verify that business logic is preserved before the final Java transformation.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Example prompt to guide the conversion:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-prompt"&gt;Generate production-ready COBOL code from these specifications.

Program Name: [PROGRAM_NAME]
Assembly Code: [PASTE HERE]
Business Rules (BRE): [PASTE HERE]
Functional Specification: [PASTE HERE]

## REQUIREMENTS
- Implement the business rules from BRE with traceability comments (BR IDs)
- Use exact data structures from specification
- Follow process flow sequence with error handling

## COBOL STRUCTURE
Include: IDENTIFICATION DIVISION (PROGRAM-ID, AUTHOR, DATE-WRITTEN), ENVIRONMENT DIVISION (if needed), DATA DIVISION (WORKING-STORAGE SECTION, FILE/LINKAGE sections if needed), PROCEDURE DIVISION (main paragraph, sub-paragraphs, STOP RUN/GOBACK)

## FORMATTING (STRICT)
Columns 1-6: Sequence numbers (000100, 000200, +100)
Column 7: Indicator (* comment, - continuation, space normal)
Columns 8-11: Area A (Division/Section headers, Paragraphs, Level 01/77)
Columns 12-72: Area B (Level 05-88, statements)

Alignment:
- Level 01/77: Column 8 | Level 05-88: Column 12
- PIC clauses: Column 40 | VALUE clauses: Column 56
- Indent subordinate levels by 4 spaces
- Paragraph names: Column 8 | Statements: Column 12
- Use explicit scope terminators (END-IF, END-PERFORM)

## DATA TYPES
Numeric: PIC 9(n)/S9(n) (integer), PIC 9(n)V9(m)/S9(n)V9(m) (decimal), COMP/BINARY (indexes), COMP-3 (financial)
Alphanumeric: PIC X(n), PIC A(n)
Condition Names: 88-level for flags

## COMMENTS
1.Header(MANDATORY):
000100*****************************************************************
000200* Program: [NAME]                                               *
000300* Purpose: [Description]                                        *
000400* Generated from Assembly using BRE transformation              *
000500* Business Rules: [COUNT] | Data Mappings: [COUNT]              *
000600*****************************************************************

2.Section comments before major sections. 
3.Business rule comments reference BR IDs:
002000* Implements BR001: Validate Input Numbers
002100 VALIDATE-INPUTS.

## IMPLEMENTATION
- Create dedicated paragraph per business rule with BR ID comment
- Implement exact logic from BRE, handle the conditions
- Follow process flow order using PERFORM statements
- Use IF/EVALUATE for decisions, PERFORM UNTIL/VARYING for loops
- Include error handling: validate inputs, check division by zero, verify bounds, set error flags

## OUTPUT FORMAT
CRITICAL: Return ONLY COBOL code. NO explanatory text, NO markdown blocks, NO commentary.
START with sequence-numbered header comment. END with STOP RUN/GOBACK.
&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You can modify the formatting requirements to match your organization’s COBOL standards. When you run this prompt, you will generate COBOL code designed to be syntactically correct, logically equivalent to the original Assembler, and fully traceable, with comments linking each code section back to specific business rules from the BRE document. Once generated, rigorously validate the COBOL code before proceeding to the final Java transformation.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="validating-the-transformation"&gt;Validating the transformation&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You should treat testing as an ongoing activity throughout the transformation process, not only a final phase. Your functional equivalence validation between Assembler and COBOL code should employ a comprehensive testing framework that systematically evaluates syntax correctness, structural alignment, logic equivalence, data flow integrity, business rules coverage, and specification compliance.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Example prompt to guide the testing:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-prompt"&gt;Perform comprehensive validation and analysis of ASM-to-COBOL translation.

Assembly Code: [PASTE HERE]
COBOL Code: [PASTE HERE]
BRE JSON: [PASTE HERE]
Spec JSON: [PASTE HERE]

## PART 1: VALIDATION

Perform 6 validation checks:

1.Syntax Check: Verify COBOL syntax (divisions, PIC clauses, statements, keywords, column alignment)
   Return: valid (true/false), errors array, warnings array
2.Structure Check: Compare program structure (sections, flow, procedures, control flow, data organization)
   Return: equivalent (true/false), score (0.0-1.0), issues array
3.Logic Check: Verify logic equivalence (conditionals, calculations, loops, error handling, edge cases)
   Return: equivalent (true/false), score (0.0-1.0), differences array
4.Data Flow Check: Verify data transformations (mappings, conversions, I/O, calculations, precision)
   Return: equivalent (true/false), score (0.0-1.0), issues array
5.BRE Coverage Check: Verify the business rules implemented (location, inputs/outputs, conditions)
   Return: coverage_score (0.0-1.0), implemented_rules array, missing_rules array, details object
6.Spec Compliance Check: Verify specification adherence (data structures, process flow, functions, data types)
   Return: compliant (true/false), score (0.0-1.0), issues array

Overall Equivalence Score: Weighted average (Syntax 20%, Structure 20%, Logic 30%, Data Flow 30%, BRE Coverage +10%, Spec Compliance +10%). Status "passed" if ≥0.7.

## PART 2: ANALYSIS

1.Quality Assessment: Classify quality (Excellent ≥0.9, Good ≥0.7, Fair ≥0.5, Poor &amp;lt;0.5) with description and validation status
2.Quality Metrics: Detailed scores for the 6 validation dimensions
3.Improvement Areas: Prioritized list with area name, severity (high/medium/low), issues array, priority (1-5)
4.Recommendations: Actionable suggestions per area (syntax fixes, structure alignment, logic verification, data flow corrections, missing BR implementation, spec compliance)
5.BRE Analysis: Per-rule analysis (rule_id, implemented, location, quality 1-5, deviations, notes) with overall_implementation_quality and summary
6.Code Suggestions: Specific improvements with category (Structure/Naming/Error Handling/Performance/Best Practices), suggestion, location, priority, optional example
7.Executive Summary: Quality level, equivalence score, validation status, improvement areas count, top 3 issues, key recommendations

## OUTPUT FORMAT

Return single JSON with validation and analysis objects. Include the fields: status, equivalence_score, validation_results (the 6 checks), findings, recommendations, quality_assessment, quality_metrics, improvement_areas, bre_analysis, code_suggestions, summary.
CRITICAL: Be thorough, specific (line numbers/paragraphs), actionable, honest (accurate scores), comprehensive (the rules/dimensions). Return JSON only, no explanatory text.
Generate complete validation and analysis report now.
&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You can customize the validation thresholds based on your quality requirements. When you run this prompt, you will receive a comprehensive validation report with actionable recommendations for addressing gaps in program structure, conditional logic, data transformations, and traceability between the original Assembler instructions and the modernized COBOL implementation.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="cobol-to-java-transformation"&gt;COBOL to Java transformation&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In the final phase, you use &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/transform/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Transform&lt;/a&gt; to automatically convert validated COBOL code into Java Spring Boot applications. Generate production-ready Java code with the intelligent refactoring in AWS Transform. This refactoring preserves the business logic validated in previous phases while introducing modern design patterns, including dependency injection, repository patterns, and service layers.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You can deploy the resulting Java applications on AWS infrastructure to achieve cloud scalability and resilience while maintaining the same business functionality as your legacy mainframe systems. This cloud-focused approach replaces monolithic mainframe architecture with modular, maintainable code that can be independently deployed, monitored, and enhanced.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For detailed step-by-step instructions, refer to the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/transform/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Transform Refactor Workshop&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="lessons-learned"&gt;Lessons learned&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;When you apply this methodology in practice, here are the key lessons that can shape your approach:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Human-in-the-loop validation is essential for transformation quality.&lt;/strong&gt; Amazon Bedrock and Kiro IDE can help improve business rule identification, but AI-generated documents should always be treated as drafts. SME review is essential at every critical stage, not only during extraction, to catch domain-specific logic, edge cases, and business context that AI tools may overlook. This human oversight can help reduce post-deployment defects compared to automated conversion without structured checkpoints.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;The intermediate COBOL step reduces risk.&lt;/strong&gt; You will find the multi-stage approach (Assembler to COBOL to Java) with validation checkpoints at each phase more reliable than attempting direct conversion. The intermediate step helps you verify business logic preservation before committing to the final Java transformation.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;A spec-driven methodology helps you prevent rework.&lt;/strong&gt; Creating detailed functional specifications before code conversion catches misunderstandings early. With structured JSON specifications, you can also automate validation and direct integration with transformation tools.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Incremental validation helps you accelerate your overall timeline.&lt;/strong&gt; Testing after each transformation stage, rather than waiting until the end, helps you detect issues early and reduces overall rework. Quality gates at each phase help confirm the output is ready for the next stage.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="conclusion-and-next-steps"&gt;Conclusion and next steps&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In this post, we walked through a systematic, AI-powered approach to transform legacy mainframe Assembler applications into Java applications built for the cloud on AWS. This approach combines Amazon Bedrock and Kiro IDE for intelligent business logic extraction with AWS Transform for automated COBOL-to-Java conversion. By following a multi-stage methodology with validation checkpoints at each phase, you can help accelerate modernization timelines while preserving critical business logic integrity.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To evaluate the full service landscape and understand how the components fit together, start with the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/mainframe-modernization/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Mainframe Modernization documentation&lt;/a&gt;. When you’re ready to see the pipeline in action, the &lt;a href="https://catalog.workshops.aws/awstransformrefactor/en-US/100-introduction" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Transform Refactor Workshop&lt;/a&gt; walks you through the end-to-end process with sample workloads you can run in your own environment. If you’d like to discuss how this approach applies to your specific environment, &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/contact-us/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;contact an AWS representative&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Tell us about your modernization journey and share your thoughts in the comments.&lt;/p&gt;

			</item>
		<item>
		<title>Novo Nordisk modernizes manufacturing process documents with Amazon Bedrock AgentCore</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/novo-nordisk-modernizes-manufacturing-process-documents-with-amazon-bedrock-agentcore/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Elias Bedmar]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Mon, 16 Nov 2026 17:04:11 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock AgentCore]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Customer Solutions]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">9189364d4fda8ced7c7c27d0b70ec7adb9ac5ce3</guid>

					<description>Novo Nordisk's Manufacturing Technology team within the Tablet area team built a process-intelligence assistant on Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, using a GraphRAG architecture that pairs a knowledge graph in Amazon Neptune with hybrid search in Amazon OpenSearch Serverless to turn millions of controlled manufacturing documents into grounded, cited answers.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://www.novonordisk.com/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Novo Nordisk&lt;/a&gt; (Novo) is one of the world’s leading global pharmaceutical companies, manufacturing medicines for chronic diseases such as diabetes and obesity, and delivering them to patients across many countries. Behind every product lies a manufacturing network that adheres to strict quality requirements. Every quality-relevant process step must be described, controlled, and validated through formal documentation. Keeping this documentation current and accurate is a core responsibility of the Manufacturing Technology team within the Tablet area, a responsibility that spans a large and diverse portfolio of controlled documents, including standard operating procedures, work instructions, forms, and training materials, across the global organization.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Managing this portfolio presents significant challenges. Document updates are frequent, driven by regulatory changes, process improvements, equipment modifications, and audit findings. Each update demands careful review, structured approval workflows, and timely communication to all affected stakeholders. With thousands of active documents spread across multiple divisions, departments, and product programs, tracking which documents are due for revision, pending approval, or require immediate attention becomes increasingly complex. Much of this oversight currently relies on manual processes and fragmented tracking methods such as spreadsheets or disconnected systems. These methods are time-consuming, error-prone, and difficult to scale. The result is a risk of outdated documentation remaining in circulation, delayed updates, and critical gaps in compliance visibility.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Beyond document management, there is a deeper knowledge challenge. Valuable manufacturing knowledge is distributed across many controlled documents, versions, sites, and product programs. Finding the right answer at the right time often depends on experienced individuals knowing where to look. This dependency on tribal knowledge creates bottlenecks that slow down decision-making and, ultimately, impact how quickly life-saving medicines reach patients.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To address these challenges, the Manufacturing Technology team within the Tablet area has embarked on developing an intelligent support system powered by agentic AI, deployed on AWS. The goal is to reduce lead times, improve documentation visibility, and make critical manufacturing knowledge more accessible, ultimately helping the organization move faster and serve more patients around the world. This post dives deep into the architecture and the key design decisions made to meet the high standards of accuracy and traceability required in a regulated pharmaceutical environment. The solution helped subject-matter experts (SMEs) complete work that used to take hours or days in minutes.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;We also describe an AI process-intelligence assistant that can answer complex process questions with grounded, cited responses. The solution is built around a GraphRAG architecture, combining a knowledge graph with hybrid search to retrieve the most relevant and contextually accurate information. AI agents, built on a managed agent framework, coordinate the retrieval and response process end-to-end. A &lt;em&gt;guided mode&lt;/em&gt; helps clarify ambiguous questions before a search is initiated, helping make responses more precise and defensible.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="the-challenge"&gt;The challenge&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Teams supporting manufacturing repeatedly answer variations of the same question: &lt;em&gt;what does the process require, for this site, this product, and this equipment?&lt;/em&gt; Answering well requires four things at once:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The right document out of a large corpus.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The right version and lifecycle state (approved and effective, not draft or obsolete).&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The right scope (it actually applies to the site, product generation, and equipment).&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The relationships (which documents reference which, and what a change would impact).&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Conventional keyword search returns documents, not answers, with no concept of scope, version, or relationship. A plain vector-based Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) system improves recall on natural-language questions but still struggles with exact identifiers (document numbers, equipment codes), with multi-hop relationship questions, and (most importantly for a regulated domain) with identifying when relevant content is not available. The team needed a system that treats structure and relationships as first-class, stays grounded in approved sources, and clarifies instead of guessing.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="solution-overview"&gt;Solution overview&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The assistant is built on a GraphRAG architecture: a knowledge graph in &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/neptune/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Neptune&lt;/a&gt; captures structure and relationships, and a hybrid search index in &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/opensearch-service/serverless/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon OpenSearch Serverless&lt;/a&gt; provides semantic and exact-match recall over document contents. The two stores are deliberately kept separate and bridged at query time rather than combined into a single engine. This is a common production GraphRAG pattern.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The reasoning layer runs on &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/agentcore/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/a&gt;, a platform to build, connect, and optimize agents at scale with any framework or model. AgentCore was chosen for its managed agent runtime with built-in session isolation, native tool orchestration, and integrated AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM) and observability. This removes undifferentiated infrastructure work so the team could focus on the domain logic. The agent runtimes are built with the &lt;a href="https://github.com/strands-agents" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Strands Agents SDK&lt;/a&gt; and powered by &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/claude/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Anthropic Claude&lt;/a&gt; models on &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt;. Documents are processed through an ingestion pipeline that uses &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/data-automation/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock Data Automation&lt;/a&gt; for multimodal parsing, orchestrated with &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/step-functions/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Step Functions&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/lambda/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Lambda&lt;/a&gt;. The stack is defined as infrastructure as code with the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/cdk/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Cloud Development Kit (AWS CDK)&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;At a high level, a question flows through the system like this:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The &lt;em&gt;query agent &lt;/em&gt;receives the question and, in guided mode, determines whether the scope is clear enough to answer well.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;If it is ambiguous, the agent asks a short, graph-grounded clarifying question (for example, which site or product generation applies) before searching.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;After scope is clear, hybrid search retrieves candidate passages, the graph expands from those results to add structure and relationships, and a reranking step sharpens precision.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The agent composes an answer grounded in and cited to approved source documents, and is explicit when content is not available.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="architecture"&gt;Architecture&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/10/ML-21325-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/10/ML-21325-1.png" alt="End-to-end architecture of the process-intelligence assistant on Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, with hybrid retrieval across Amazon OpenSearch Serverless and Amazon Neptune" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 1: End-to-end architecture where a query agent on Amazon Bedrock AgentCore uses Amazon Bedrock for reasoning, embeddings, and reranking, with hybrid retrieval across Amazon OpenSearch Serverless and Amazon Neptune linked by shared document numbers. An event-driven, serverless ingestion pipeline populates both stores, while memory, observability, and evaluations support personalization, monitoring, and quality assessment&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="a-two-layer-taxonomy-structure-plus-domain-detail"&gt;A two-layer taxonomy: Structure plus domain detail&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The graph is organized around two complementary layers, which is what lets the assistant take into account &lt;em&gt;where&lt;/em&gt; a document sits and &lt;em&gt;what&lt;/em&gt; it is about.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;High-level taxonomy: The process tree.&lt;/strong&gt; The organization’s official process framework is modeled as a hierarchy of process nodes (roughly three levels, parent to child). Each controlled document is attached to the lowest applicable process level, so each onboarded document has a known place. This lets the assistant scope a question to a process area before retrieving anything, and automatically categorize newly ingested documents.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Low-level taxonomy: Domain entities.&lt;/strong&gt; SMEs define the entities that make an answer correct or incorrect in manufacturing: sites, product generations, equipment, process steps, and similar attributes. These are modeled as first-class graph nodes linked to the documents that describe them, using a small set of unified relationship types rather than an ever-growing list of edge names.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Modeling both layers is what distinguishes a genuine GraphRAG system from &lt;em&gt;two databases side by side&lt;/em&gt;. The document number on an OpenSearch passage is also a node in the graph, so a search hit is an immediate entry point into the graph’s structure with no fuzzy re-matching required. This chunk-to-graph bridge is a key design choice in the retrieval path.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="why-a-fixed-curated-taxonomy-and-why-this-is-best-practice"&gt;Why a fixed, curated taxonomy (and why this is best practice)&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Early GraphRAG work favored open-ended extraction, letting a language model discover entities with no fixed schema. This approach is powerful for open-ended &lt;em&gt;sensemaking&lt;/em&gt; over sprawling text, but it is noisy, hard to maintain, and difficult to audit. Over the past couple of years, the consensus has shifted toward ontology-guided extraction using a predefined, domain-specific schema to guide the model, which improves accuracy and reliability for specialized corpora.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For regulated pharmaceutical manufacturing this is not a compromise, but the right default. The domain already has a real ontology, curated by experts, and a fixed schema with a human-in-the-loop promotion process for new entities supports the auditability the team needed in a regulated pharmaceutical environment. The design aligns with where much of the field has moved since early open-schema approaches, while making design decisions easier to explain to quality and audit stakeholders.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="retrieval-hybrid-search-graph-expansion-and-reranking"&gt;Retrieval: Hybrid search, graph expansion, and reranking&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Each store covers the other’s blind spot:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;OpenSearch provides recall –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;Hybrid search combines dense vector similarity (Amazon Titan Text Embeddings) with lexical BM25 matching, fused with reciprocal rank fusion. Vector search captures meaning. And lexical matching catches the exact tokens that matter here: document numbers, equipment codes, and multilingual terminology.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Neptune provides structure –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;Given the entities and documents search surfaced, the graph answers “what is connected, how, and how far”: neighboring documents, references, position in the hierarchy, and impact of a change, which is exactly what vector-only retrieval cannot do.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;A common pattern is &lt;em&gt;vector finds the seeds, the graph expands from them,&lt;/em&gt; and the reverse for relationship-first questions. A &lt;em&gt;reranking&lt;/em&gt; stage (a cross-encoder through Amazon Bedrock Rerank) sits after fusion: it reads the question and each candidate passage together and reorders them, adding a precision pass on top of the cheaper recall stage before anything reaches the model.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="guided-mode-clarify-before-you-retrieve"&gt;Guided mode: Clarify before you retrieve&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;A distinctive part of the assistant is how it handles ambiguity. A question like &lt;em&gt;“which samples are taken on the granulator?”&lt;/em&gt; has several correct answers depending on product generation, site, equipment, and batch size. Answering immediately risks a confident, wrong, out-of-scope response, which is the worst outcome in a regulated setting.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Guided mode inverts the usual RAG order: instead of retrieving first, the agent narrows scope through clarification first, then searches. This follows recent research on clarifying ambiguous questions before retrieval and on using a knowledge graph to &lt;em&gt;supply&lt;/em&gt; the clarification options rather than letting the model invent them. It uses two classes of scope:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;A small, near-universal set of attributes (such as site and product generation) is asked directly and consistently for process-specific questions.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Everything deeper: which equipment, which batch size, and attributes added over time. This is all derived from the graph at query time. A scope-resolution step matches the user’s term against real graph nodes, filters by what is already known, and, if more than one candidate remains, offers the attribute that genuinely distinguishes them, using only values that exist in the graph.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This keeps clarification options grounded in real data (designed to help reduce the risk of hallucinated options) and it scales: when experts add a new distinguishing attribute to the taxonomy, the assistant surfaces it automatically, without requiring changes to prompts or code.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="grounding-and-trust-in-a-regulated-environment"&gt;Grounding and trust in a regulated environment&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Accuracy here is defined narrowly and enforced deliberately:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Explicit indexed state –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;A document can exist in the graph with metadata, relationships, and place in the hierarchy before its contents are searchable. A deterministic flag tracks whether a document’s content is indexed, so the assistant can distinguish between “I have this document’s contents” and “I only have its metadata,” rather than inferring availability from empty search results.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Approved sources by default –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;Answers rely on approved and effective documents and the latest applicable version, unless the user explicitly asks for drafts or historical revisions.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Anti-hallucination rules –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;Clarification options and cited claims are designed to come from actual tool results rather than the model’s own knowledge.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Stated limitations –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;When retrieved material is missing or ambiguous, the assistant says so rather than filling the gap and flags when SME, quality, or regulatory review is needed.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;An automated evaluation harness checks responses against these criteria: relevant-source selection, valid document status, latest applicable version, scope compliance, citation accuracy, and faithful preservation of requirement strength (must, should, or may) so behavior can be measured as the system evolves.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="an-intelligent-search-engine-over-a-vault-of-millions-of-documents"&gt;An intelligent search engine over a vault of millions of documents&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The wider vault this solution sits on top of holds on the order of four million documents. At that scale, the problem is not a shortage of information but the near-impossibility of finding the exactly right, in-scope, current answer by hand. Traditional enterprise search returns long lists ranked by keyword overlap and leaves the hard work to the person asking. This includes deciding which result applies, which version is effective, and how documents relate.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This solution turns the vault into an intelligent, answer-first search engine, and the two-layer taxonomy is what makes that tractable at scale:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;The graph is the full map and the index is the searchable subset.&lt;/strong&gt; Each onboarded document across all registered domains is placed in the graph with its metadata, relationships, and hierarchy position. This means the assistant can reason about where a document sits and how it connects, even for the millions whose contents are not yet indexed. Full-text contents are indexed for onboarded domains, added incrementally, and the assistant is explicit about which is which.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Scope narrows the search space before retrieval.&lt;/strong&gt; Because guided mode resolves site, product generation, process area, and equipment up front, a question is filtered to the small slice of the vault that can answer it. The system searches the few documents in scope, not millions. This helps keep answers precise as the corpus grows.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Hybrid search plus graph expansion goes beyond keyword ranking&lt;/strong&gt;, surfacing related documents, references, and hierarchy a ranked list would never reveal, and returning a grounded, cited answer (not ten blue links) while saying when content is unavailable.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The design is intended to scale with the vault rather than degrade as it grows: the larger and more interconnected the documentation, the more value the graph and scoping add.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="outcomes"&gt;Outcomes&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Depending on the type of task an SME is doing (searching for an answer, updating a document, or adapting one for a new context), work that used to take hours or days is reduced to minutes. Beyond raw time saved, the solution changes what is practical to do at all:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Navigate the group’s process landscape –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;Users can move across all three levels of the process taxonomy, spanning roughly four million documents and their many subprocesses through a single assistant that acts as both a search engine and a guide over the documentation, the process, and the organizational maps.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Consolidate structured and unstructured data –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;Metadata, process hierarchy, and document contents are unified into one searchable knowledge graph, so information that used to live in separate systems can be queried together.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Understand relationships between entities and documents –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;The graph helps users ask how documents, processes, equipment, sites, and products connect, including multi-hop questions that manual search cannot answer.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Trace and validate references end to end –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;The solution can map a document’s full chain of references and flag where a document points at out-of-date or superseded material, surfacing integrity issues across the corpus rather than one document at a time.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Extend to further AI and agentic capabilities –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;The same grounded foundation opens the door to capabilities like document generation, revision with impact analysis, and other agentic workflows reusing the same graph, retrieval, and guardrails rather than starting from scratch.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Just as important in a regulated setting, the assistant’s design to clarify scope and flag missing or ambiguous content helps reduce the risk of confident, out-of-scope answers, where the real cost of a mistake lies.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="a-collaborative-delivery-model"&gt;A collaborative delivery model&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The Novo Manufacturing Technology team within the Tablet area collaborated with AWS to build this solution, with subject-matter experts and data and AI engineers working closely throughout. The taxonomy that makes the assistant accurate comes directly from the group’s domain experts. The retrieval and agent architecture was designed and iterated with AWS.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Building a prototype was key to getting the solution to go live. Rather than debating capabilities in the abstract, the team put a working system in front of subject-matter experts early. They tested it against real questions and used what they learned to sharpen the taxonomy, the retrieval path, and the guided-mode behavior. The prototype turned an ambitious idea into something people could try, critique, and trust. It also de-risked the architecture decisions before any broader rollout.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="conclusion"&gt;Conclusion&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;By combining a curated knowledge graph, hybrid search, and a clarify-before-retrieve agent on Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, the Novo Manufacturing Technology team within the Tablet area built a process-intelligence assistant that is fast where speed helps and grounded where accuracy matters. The design applies current GraphRAG patterns to regulated manufacturing: ontology-guided extraction, a bridged two-store retrieval path, graph-grounded disambiguation, and reranking. This turns a large documentation landscape into grounded, cited answers with room to extend to new domains.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To get started building your own agentic AI solution, explore &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/agentcore/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/a&gt; for managed agent runtime, tool orchestration, and observability at scale. The agent runtimes in this post were built with the &lt;a href="https://github.com/strands-agents" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Strands Agents SDK&lt;/a&gt;, an open source, framework agnostic toolkit you can use with your own retrieval stack. For GraphRAG reference architectures using Amazon Neptune and Amazon OpenSearch Serverless, visit the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/architecture/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Architecture Center&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;

					<description><![CDATA[<p>Our new integrations let AI agents reason across Oracle’s business-critical data.</p>
<p>The post <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/microsoft-and-oracle-bring-agentic-ai-into-flow-of-enterprise-work/">Microsoft and Oracle bring agentic AI into flow of enterprise work</a> appeared first on <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog">Microsoft Azure Blog</a>.</p>
]]></description>
										<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><em>This blog is co-authored by Arpan Shah, SVP of Oracle Databases and Muneer Mirza, VP of Multicloud at Oracle Cloud Infrastructure (OCI).</em></p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Today at Microsoft Ignite, we’re bringing agentic AI into the flow of enterprise work. Our new integrations let AI agents reason across Oracle’s business-critical data—from finance and supply chain to HR and customer operations—and the work context Microsoft Work IQ understands across meetings, email, documents, and Teams. Customers can access this combined intelligence through Excel, Teams, and Microsoft Copilot. Built on <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/solutions/oracle/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Oracle AI Database@Azure</a>, the integrations help businesses turn trusted enterprise data into insights and actions, with the choice, security, and performance mission-critical workloads demand.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/solutions/oracle/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Learn more about Oracle on Azure</a></div>
</div>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">With Oracle AI Database@Azure, Oracle and Microsoft have helped customers modernize critical workloads to improve application performance and unlock more value from their enterprise data, by bringing databases closer to applications and connecting enterprise data with Microsoft AI. Oracle AI Database@Azure now spans 33 Azure regions, more than on any other hyperscaler, delivering the performance and flexibility customers need.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">At Oracle AI World 2026, we announced new capabilities for Oracle AI Database@Azure including FedRAMP Class D certification coming soon for US Government and public-sector workloads, and Proximity Placement Groups (PPGs) that enable customers to place their database closer to applications—resulting in approximately 50% lower VM-to-Exadata network round-trip latency than deployments without PPG alignment. Now, at Microsoft Ignite 2026, we’re building on that momentum.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="for-it-operations-monitoring-and-trust-at-every-layer">For IT operations: Monitoring and trust at every layer</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Today, we’re announcing limited preview of Azure Workload Center for Oracle, bringing each workload’s architecture, resources, and dependencies together through a construct called a Workload Virtual Instance (WVI). This significantly reduces the complexity and resources required to manage solutions at the individual component level. Integrations across the Workload Virtual Instance, Azure Monitor Health Models, and the <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Azure</a> Copilot Run Oracle agent connect monitoring signals across application, database, and infrastructure layers.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">This operational visibility is complemented by agent access controls: Oracle AI Database@Azure supports Microsoft Entra authentication and Oracle Deep Data Security, helping agents stay within users’ existing permissions while database calls run through the managed model context protocol (MCP) server and activity remains auditable across your Oracle database estate.</p>


<figure data-wp-context="{&quot;imageId&quot;:&quot;6afcf4d812f02&quot;}" data-wp-interactive="core/image" data-wp-key="6afcf4d812f02" class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-full wp-lightbox-container"><img decoding="async" data-wp-class--hide="state.isContentHidden" data-wp-class--show="state.isContentVisible" data-wp-init="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on--click="actions.showLightbox" data-wp-on--load="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on-window--resize="callbacks.setButtonStyles" src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/azure_workload_health_upscaled.webp" alt="Azure Workload Center for Oracle showing degraded workload health for the Caldova Oracle workload. Copilot summarizes the issue and identifies database services as the cause, while the Run Oracle agent provides workload-aware troubleshooting and resolution options." class="wp-image-55525 webp-format" srcset="" data-orig-src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/azure_workload_health_upscaled.webp"><button

			</svg>
