<p class="wp-block-paragraph">New models ship every few weeks, and your customers ask questions no one planned for. The agents that learn from all of it pull ahead. That’s continuous improvement, and today at Microsoft Ignite we’re bringing it to Microsoft Foundry. To unlock it, you need an <a href="https://blogs.microsoft.com/blog/2026/06/02/ai-alone-wont-change-your-business-the-system-running-it-will/" id="https://blogs.microsoft.com/blog/2026/06/02/ai-alone-wont-change-your-business-the-system-running-it-will/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">end-to-end system</a> where you build agents, give them context, run them, govern them, and improve them over time.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">And as part of the new <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/ai/microsoft-iq" id="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/ai/microsoft-iq" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft IQ</a>, Foundry accomplishes two things that only Microsoft can deliver: your unique intelligence stays your advantage, and your agents keep getting better the longer they run.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Out of the box, an AI model is exactly the same for you as it is for anyone else. Three things help you improve your agents as your teams accomplish real work.</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Agents on Foundry get better the more you use them.</strong> Foundry lets you feed agent traces and user input back into the system, creating a continuous learning loop that improves the performance of the system over time.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Foundry looks across every run for ways to make your agents better.</strong> The system finds and tests improvements; shows you how each one trades off across quality, speed, and cost; and then you decide what ships.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Foundry provides a range of other capabilities that allow you to improve progressively,</strong> from lightweight skill improvements to agent configuration, all the way to training model weights using your data.</li>
</ul>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Underneath it all, you have <a href="https://github.com/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">GitHub</a>, where you build your agents, and <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/ai-foundry" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Foundry</a>, where you run and supervise them as they continuously improve. And they both run on Azure as one system that operates on a global scale.<a id="_msocom_1"></a><a id="_msocom_12"></a></p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://ai.azure.com/home" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">See what&#8217;s new in Foundry today</a></div>
</div>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="github-building-intelligent-agents">GitHub: Building intelligent agents</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Before you improve an agent, you need to build it. Say you want an agent to automate your supply chain processes, and you want that agent to be available in Teams—where your team already works. Just explain what you’re trying to do in clear, everyday language, and <a href="https://github.com/features/copilot" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">GitHub Copilot</a> writes most, if not all, of the code. And with <a href="https://github.blog/changelog/2026-10-28-route-and-orchestrate-models-with-github-copilot-fusion/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">GitHub Copilot Fusion</a> under the hood, it routes and orchestrates models while you’re building, so you don’t have to. That means you get the right model for the job, while balancing performance, latency, and cost.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="foundry-running-and-continuously-improving-agents-at-scale">Foundry: Running and continuously improving agents at scale</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Once you’ve built your agent, you need a place to run it, govern it, and improve it for all your teams, in every region, alongside hundreds or thousands of other agents.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/ai-foundry/models" id="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/ai-foundry/models" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">With more than 11,000 models</a>, from OpenAI and Anthropic to Meta, Mistral, open source, our own Microsoft AI (MAI) models, and more, Foundry lets you do just that. And you don’t have to choose just one or be locked into a single model provider. Foundry’s model router sends simple requests to small, fast models and more complicated asks to larger, state-of-the-art models, so you spend less on tokens, time, and compute. Our testing shows that using Foundry’s model router can save you up to 65% compared with sending everything to the frontier.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">When your agent runs in Foundry, it has an identity. Entra Agent ID and <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/guardrails/guardrails-overview" id="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/guardrails/guardrails-overview" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Foundry guardrails</a> give the agent only the access it needs, and sensitive actions keep a human in the loop as required by your business. And your organization stays in control at every step. You set the standard, every change is tested against evidence, and your team decides what reaches production. Agent controls in Foundry Safety extend that oversight to the runtime. For agents built in Foundry and, in the future, outside it, this means checking an agent’s behavior against the guardrail policies you set as it runs.</p>



<div class="alignright wp-block-bloginabox-theme-kicker" data-bi-an="Kicker Right">
	<div class="kicker">
		<h2 class="kicker__title">
			ignite session: The trust foundation&#8230;		</h2>
		<p class="kicker__content">
							<a
					href="https://ignite.microsoft.com/en-US/sessions/BRK312"
					class="kicker__link"
					target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"				>
						Watch now							↗</a>
					</p>
	</div>
</div>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Run agents with the orchestration layer of your choice. <a href="https://aka.ms/Ignite26agentsdevblog" id="https://aka.ms/Ignite26agentsdevblog" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Foundry Agent Service</a> runs agents built with your preferred framework and harness through <strong>hosted agents</strong>, which now handle more concurrent sessions at once (in general availability [GA]) and start faster (in preview). And for teams that would rather have the harness managed for them, the <a href="https://blogs.microsoft.com/blog/2026/10/28/introducing-the-microsoft-copilot-harness-unlocking-the-potential-of-truly-multi-model-agentic-work/" id="https://blogs.microsoft.com/blog/2026/10/28/introducing-the-microsoft-copilot-harness-unlocking-the-potential-of-truly-multi-model-agentic-work/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"><strong>Microsoft Copilot harness</strong></a> (now in preview) has built-in capabilities like reusable skills and sub-agents. <a href="https://aka.ms/Ignite26agentsfashable" id="https://aka.ms/Ignite26agentsfashable" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Fashable, an AI-native fashion company, is using <strong>voice agents</strong> (now in GA) and the Microsoft Copilot harness</a> to make design and sourcing more conversational and agent powered.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4vCbQMUVV-I" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"><strong>Watch the Microsoft Mechanics episode about the Microsoft Copilot harness</strong></a></div>
</div>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Foundry learns from how your teams use your agents, evaluates every change against your own standard of what “good” looks like, and helps you make each agent better. Agents learn from their own past work and your experts’ feedback, and those lessons become new skills, evaluations, and more. That’s how your agents, and the system around them, keep getting better.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="how-continuous-improvement-works">How continuous improvement works </h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Continuous improvement in <a href="https://ai.azure.com/home" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Foundry</a> follows four steps, repeated, so each pass builds on the last: <strong>Establish</strong>, <strong>Identify</strong>, <strong>Optimize</strong>, then <strong>Validate and repeat</strong>.</p>


<figure data-wp-context="{&quot;imageId&quot;:&quot;6afcf4d82a9f9&quot;}" data-wp-interactive="core/image" data-wp-key="6afcf4d82a9f9" class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-large wp-lightbox-container"><img decoding="async" data-wp-class--hide="state.isContentHidden" data-wp-class--show="state.isContentVisible" data-wp-init="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on--click="actions.showLightbox" data-wp-on--load="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on-window--resize="callbacks.setButtonStyles" src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/Continuous_improvement_visual_simplified-1024x696.webp" alt="Graphic showing four-step continuous improvement cycle in Microsoft Foundry." class="wp-image-55516 webp-format" srcset="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/Continuous_improvement_visual_simplified-1024x696.webp 1024w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/Continuous_improvement_visual_simplified-300x204.webp 300w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/Continuous_improvement_visual_simplified-768x522.webp 768w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/Continuous_improvement_visual_simplified-1536x1044.webp 1536w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/Continuous_improvement_visual_simplified-2048x1393.webp 2048w" data-orig-src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/Continuous_improvement_visual_simplified-1024x696.webp"><button
			class="lightbox-trigger"
			type="button"
			aria-haspopup="dialog"

<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="establish-you-decide-what-good-looks-like">Establish: You decide what good looks like</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Improvement starts with a clear standard. In Foundry, that standard lives in evaluations, which are automated tests that score an agent’s responses against criteria you choose. <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/concepts/evaluation-evaluators/rubric-evaluators" id="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/concepts/evaluation-evaluators/rubric-evaluators" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"><strong>Rubric evaluators</strong></a> let you write those criteria for your own business, like a checklist your best expert would use, and they now cover safety as well as quality (in preview).</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Your people know quality better than any metric, and their feedback turns a capable agent into an expert on your business. Every time an agent runs, Foundry logs a trace: a step-by-step record of what the agent was asked, what it did, and how it answered. With <strong>annotations on traces</strong> (now in preview), experts review real conversations and mark what worked, what didn’t, and what the agent should have done instead. Those annotated traces become <strong>evaluation datasets</strong> (in preview), built from real usage rather than hypothetical test cases. From those datasets, Foundry can generate a rubric for each test case, so your experts’ judgment becomes part of how every future version of the agent is scored.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">You can then lock in your best set of test cases as a <strong>golden dataset</strong> (in preview). Think of it as the answer key: the benchmark each new version has to beat.<a id="_msocom_1"></a></p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="identify-find-areas-where-your-agent-can-improve">Identify: Find areas where your agent can improve</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Your customers shouldn’t be the first to tell you something broke. Traces show how an agent really behaves, including where things go wrong, but there are far too many to read on your own. <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/observability/how-to/agent-insights" id="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/observability/how-to/agent-insights" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"><strong>Insights</strong></a> (now in GA) groups the problems that keep recurring, shows the evidence, and points to a likely cause, so teams fix what affects the most people first. And your teams can bring the same visibility to agents built outside Foundry with <strong>LangGraph, LangChain, and CrewAI</strong> (now in GA).</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="optimize-make-a-good-agent-better">Optimize: Make a good agent better</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><strong>Agent optimizer</strong> turns what you’ve learned into a better agent, whether you’re fixing a repeated mistake or raising the bar on an agent that already performs well. It improves an agent’s instructions, skills, and model choice and tests every candidate change against your evaluations before anything ships. And while it recommends the strongest candidate, your team decides which change to ship—and nothing changes in production until you apply it. Agent optimizer builds on prompt optimization and <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/openai/concepts/model-router" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Foundry’s model router</a> (both already available), with new ways to improve an agent:</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Sending insights to agent optimizer</strong> (now in preview) turns a problem found in production into a fix in one step. Pick a recurring issue in insights, send it to agent optimizer, and get candidate fixes tested against your evaluations.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Skill optimization</strong> (now in preview) helps agents learn from experience. Agent optimizer reviews an agent’s past work and the feedback your experts left, finds the mistakes it keeps repeating and the approaches that worked, and turns those lessons into new or improved skills for similar situations. Skills are versioned, so you can roll back or reuse a proven skill in other agents, and you choose when to adopt it.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Cost optimization</strong> (now in preview) finds cost-efficient ways to run your agent, like using a smaller model, without compromising on quality.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Managed Training</strong> (previously called managed fine-tuning) lets you train models on your own data. Docusign, which already cut AI processing costs by 90% with fine-tuned models in Foundry, is now fine-tuning MAI models with Managed Training to read agreements. </li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Programmed Training</strong> (now in preview) lets your engineers write their own training recipes in Python while Foundry runs them on serverless GPUs. Heidi, an AI care partner for clinicians, used Programmed Training to post-train an open-weight model for clinical notes that matches a widely used frontier model on its clinical quality metrics, at an estimated 87% lower cost per note.</li>
</ul>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Alaska Airlines put continuous improvement in Foundry to work. They explored how fine-tuning MAI-Code-2-Flash could assist trained personnel in classifying aircraft maintenance reports by ATA code, with qualified employees retaining responsibility for review and final decisions. In Alaska’s classification evaluation, the model scored 81.2% out of the box and 91.1% after tuning, outperforming GPT-6 Astra and GPT-6.1 Sol at a fraction of the cost. </p>



<figure data-wp-context="{&quot;imageId&quot;:&quot;6afcf4d82b6b3&quot;}" data-wp-interactive="core/image" data-wp-key="6afcf4d82b6b3" class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-full wp-lightbox-container"><img decoding="async" width="1120" height="630" data-wp-class--hide="state.isContentHidden" data-wp-class--show="state.isContentVisible" data-wp-init="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on--click="actions.showLightbox" data-wp-on--load="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on-window--resize="callbacks.setButtonStyles" src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/AgentOptimizer-Blog-GIF.gif" alt="Microsoft Foundry Agent Optimizer showing optimization runs that improve agent quality and cost." class="wp-image-55536" /><button

<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="validate-and-repeat-prove-the-improvement">Validate and repeat: Prove the improvement</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Every improvement should be something you can prove. When you compare a new version of an agent with your current one, <a href="https://ai.azure.com/home" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Foundry</a> runs statistical tests on the results, so you can see whether a difference is real or just noise. Teams can build the same check into the release pipeline their developers already use, in GitHub Actions or Azure DevOps, so nothing ships until it clears your quality bar.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Continuous evaluation (already available in Foundry) samples live traffic and scores it with the same evaluators you set at the start, to check that an improvement holds up with real users in production. Scheduled evaluations rerun your golden dataset, and scheduled red-team scans keep testing for new risks. Each agent’s <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/observability/how-to/how-to-monitor-agents-dashboard?tabs=python" id="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/observability/how-to/how-to-monitor-agents-dashboard?tabs=python" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">monitoring dashboard</a> shows quality, safety, latency, and token usage in one view, and alerts flag when scores slip. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">When something does slip, whether from a model update, a change in your business, or a new type of request, that signal becomes the next insight, and the loop starts again. The version you just shipped becomes the new baseline, so every round starts from your best agent to date. </p>

<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Customers are already putting this loop to work, including NTT DATA, which is <a href="https://ignite.microsoft.com/en-US/sessions/BRK249" id="https://ignite.microsoft.com/en-US/sessions/BRK249" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">sharing its story at Ignite (BRK249)</a>. NTT DATA is applying a continuous improvement loop to its program-management agent, using observability, evaluations, and optimization to identify issues and systematically improve quality. The team is validating the approach as it prepares to scale the agent across more than 1,000 internal projects, showing how continuous improvement can help maintain agent quality at enterprise scale. </p>

<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="agents-that-increase-your-advantage">Agents that increase your advantage </h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">An agent becomes an expert when it understands your business: how your people work, your organization’s institutional knowledge, and what “good” looks like—in your specific business context. Microsoft IQ grounds agents in your company’s data, know-how, and tools through a context layer you own, built from the IT investments you’ve already made. Your agents start with years of experience, and everything they learn in <a href="https://ai.azure.com/home" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Foundry</a> adds to it. That value accrues to you and stays in your business.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">We believe AI should increase your advantage. And with continuous improvement in Foundry, those gains compound over time. </p>

<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="also-announced-at-microsoft-ignite">Also announced at Microsoft Ignite </h2>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Voice agents</strong> (now in GA) let people talk to an agent the way they would talk to a colleague, by phone or in an app. </li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Microsoft 365 accounts for agents</strong> (in preview) let agents work inside the same tools your people use.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Deploying hosted agents to Azure Kubernetes Service</strong> is coming soon (in preview).</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Updates to Toolboxes</strong> and <strong>browser automation</strong> connect agents to more tools and workflows. </li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Cost in Microsoft Foundry</strong> (now in preview) gives you one view of spend across agents, models, and tools, with a forecast and month-end report on the assets driving the most cost. </li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>New Microsoft AI models</strong> for <strong>coding</strong>, <strong>security</strong>, and <strong>creative work</strong> are available, plus partner models through fal and Baseten on Foundry. </li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><a href="https://commandline.microsoft.com/microsoft-decision-model-ga/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>Microsoft-Decision-1.1</strong></a> is now generally available in Foundry and also available to be used as a judge in Foundry Evaluations to measure performance, quality, and safety. </li>
</ul>

<p>The post <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/introducing-the-new-microsoft-iq-putting-your-organizations-intelligence-to-work-in-every-agent/">Introducing the new Microsoft IQ: Putting your organization&#8217;s intelligence to work in every agent</a> appeared first on <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog">Microsoft Azure Blog</a>.</p>
]]></description>
										<content:encoded><![CDATA[

<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Microsoft believes that AI should enhance your advantage, not erode it. Every organization has access to the same frontier models, and those models improve every few weeks. Today, we are announcing <a href="https://aka.ms/microsoftiq" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>Microsoft IQ</strong></a>, the enterprise context management platform that helps organizations create their own IQ. What sets an organization apart is everything around the model: its own intelligence. That includes how its people and teams work, its business processes, institutional knowledge across the enterprise, and data stored across their systems of record including customer relationship management systems.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://aka.ms/microsoftiq" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Learn more about Microsoft IQ</a></div>
</div>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">This intelligence is the organization’s own IQ. It is what gives the organization a unique advantage; it&#8217;s what makes an agent useful and trusted, under the rules of the organization.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Microsoft IQ enables teams to curate and activate their know-how, data, skills, and tools, and create unique AI advantage that compounds over time. It brings together Work IQ, Fabric IQ, Foundry IQ, and Web IQ, in addition to more than 350 third-party connectors to create an organization’s enterprise context.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Organizations can now make this context and expertise reusable through new <strong>business</strong> <strong>plugins</strong>. Business plugins let you curate and govern the context, skills, and authoritative sources every agent inherits, allowing teams to capture their unique business processes and know-how in their agentic workflows, and creating their own IQ. A manufacturer can give agents what they need for predictive maintenance and quality root-cause analysis. An energy operator can ground control-room incident investigations. A supply chain team can speed up supplier risk and disruption response. In every case, one governed plugin is reused across agents, teams, and platforms. Users can build business plugins wherever they run their agents including Microsoft Copilot, GitHub Copilot, Microsoft Foundry through Model Context Protocol (MCP), and third-party assistants including ChatGPT and Claude marketplaces.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">As part of Microsoft IQ, we are also bringing together capabilities from Microsoft Fabric, Foundry, and GitHub to build, run, and continuously improve the intelligence that makes agents work, and to distribute that intelligence across the organization. The platform is open and model-diverse by design, built for heterogeneous enterprise environments. That means improvements made for one business process become assets your organization can apply to the next update.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="how-microsoft-iq-turns-intelligence-into-advantage">How Microsoft IQ turns intelligence into advantage</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Microsoft IQ brings together four complementary IQs: <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-365/copilot/extensibility/work-iq/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>Work IQ</strong></a> provides context on how people work, including documents, conversations, collaboration, and past decisions. <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/fabric/iq/overview" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>Fabric IQ</strong></a> provides the state of the business through governed data, business entities, relationships, and rules. <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/agents/concepts/what-is-foundry-iq?tabs=portal" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>Foundry IQ</strong></a> provides curated institutional knowledge, including policies and authoritative documents. <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/web-iq/overview" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>Web IQ</strong></a> provides timely, relevant context from the web, including news, images, and videos.</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="what-s-new-in-work-iq">What’s new in Work IQ</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en/copilot/features/work-iq" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Work IQ</a> moves from understanding work to getting it done. <strong>Business Applications in Work IQ</strong>, now generally available, adds customer relationship management (CRM) and enterprise resource planning (ERP) records to the grounding set, giving agents a fuller view of the business. <strong>Retrieve</strong>, now generally available, returns grounded, permission-trimmed Microsoft 365 content and federated connector data in a single call. <strong>Real-time meeting context</strong>, now generally available, enables agents to act on meeting content while the meeting is still in progress.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><strong>Long-running tasks and agent-to-agent support</strong>, also generally available, enable agents to manage multi-step work and ask clarifying questions along the way. <strong>Granular MCP policies</strong> (generally available) scope access by path, audience, and trusted app rather than relying on a tenant-wide switch. <strong>OpenAI and Anthropic plugins</strong> (generally available) extend Work IQ into the ChatGPT and Claude ecosystems.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">In public preview, Memory, Word, Excel, and PowerPoint creation and editing, Listen event subscriptions, and agent policy governance with human approval help agents remember what matters, create real Office content, respond when something happens, and pause for approval before consequential actions.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Read more about what is new in Work IQ in Amanda Silver’s blog, <a href="https://aka.ms/WorkIQIgnite2026" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">What&#8217;s new in Work IQ at Ignite 2026: agents that get work done</a>.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://www.microsoft.com/en/copilot/features/work-iq" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Learn more about Work IQ</a></div>
</div>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="what-s-new-in-fabric-iq">What’s new in Fabric IQ</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-fabric/features/iq" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Fabric IQ</a> brings trusted business context into more of the experiences people and AI use every day. Power BI semantic models in Microsoft Copilot are already generally available, and <strong>Fabric data agents</strong> <strong>are coming to Microsoft Copilot</strong>, with general availability coming soon.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">In Excel, <strong>Fabric IQ in Edit with Copilot</strong> is now in preview, enabling people to ask questions of governed Power BI semantic models and create live, Fabric-connected PivotTables. Also in preview, new <strong>Fabric IQ connectors for ChatGPT and Claude</strong> bring insights from Power BI semantic models into the apps people use every day, while <strong>Fabric IQ ontologies in GitHub Copilot</strong> give developers access to governed business context as they work.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">With <strong>Fabric apps</strong>, now in preview, developers can embed conversational experiences powered by Power BI semantic models and Fabric data agents into their custom applications. <strong>IQ sharing</strong> now supports <strong>ontologies in Fabric IQ</strong> in preview, so organizations can share business context with customers and partners.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Read more about what is new in Fabric IQ in Nellie Gustafsson’s blog, <a href="https://aka.ms/FabricIQ-Ignite26" id="https://aka.ms/FabricIQ-Ignite26" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Bringing trusted Fabric IQ context to wherever people and agents work </a>and read more about what’s new in Microsoft OneLake and IQ Sharing in Dipti Borkar’s blog, <a href="https://aka.ms/OneLakeIgnite26" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">What’s new in OneLake and IQ Sharing</a>.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-fabric/features/iq" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Learn more about Fabric IQ</a></div>
</div>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="what-s-new-in-foundry-iq">What’s new in Foundry IQ</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/ai-foundry/iq/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Foundry IQ</a> helps agents get more accurate answers from enterprise knowledge with fewer tokens. <strong>Knowledge graphs in Foundry IQ</strong>, now in preview, enable agents to reason across relationships in organizational knowledge in unstructured data and applications. <strong>Microsoft Retriever 1</strong>, also in preview, is a new retrieval model that enhances results for agentic grounding.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The <strong>SharePoint (indexed) knowledge source is generally available with permissions support</strong>, alongside other knowledge sources builders have been asking for, to bring more enterprise knowledge to production applications.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Generally available in December 2026, Foundry IQ Serverless offers usage-based pricing with no capacity to provision or manage, so teams pay for the knowledge their agents use, not for capacity they reserve. Free monthly allowances make it easy to start, and usage grows with your agents from prototype to production.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Read more about what is new in Foundry IQ in Pablo Castro’s blog, <a href="https://aka.ms/Ignite26FIQ" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Foundry IQ: Introducing knowledge graphs, engineered for agents</a>.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/ai-foundry/iq/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Learn more about Foundry IQ</a></div>
</div>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="what-s-new-in-web-iq">What’s new in Web IQ</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/webiq" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Web IQ</a> is now <strong>available to every Azure customer</strong>, with no waitlist and pricing starting at $1 per 1,000 calls. It adds <strong>Auto and Auto Advanced</strong> modes that can simplify web grounding by unifying Web, Images, Videos, News, Places (new), and Weather (new), while improving retrieval for complex questions. The result is less development effort and lower cost.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Web IQ provides intelligence across 207.5 million real-world entities and <a href="https://forecastwatch.com/awards/2026/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">industry-leading weather data</a>. Through Commerce, now in private preview, it also provides structured product data spanning more than 40 billion listings and offers—helping customers build context-aware experiences faster and respond with greater confidence.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><strong>New enterprise controls</strong> include Scopes, which let organizations include or exclude specific sites and domains, and Bring Your Own License, which combines licensed publisher content with web results.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Read more about what is new in Web IQ in Jordi Ribas&#8217;s blog, <a href="https://aka.ms/webiqnews" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Web IQ is now broadly available: build better agents that keep up with the world</a>.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Together, the four IQs create the enterprise context and a governed understanding of your organization, so you don’t have to explain how your business works every time you build an agent.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/webiq" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Learn more about Web IQ</a></div>
</div>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="customer-spotlight-aemo-uses-microsoft-iq-to-keep-australia-s-grid-secure">Customer spotlight: AEMO uses Microsoft IQ to keep Australia’s grid secure</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">When every second counts, AEMO relies on NEM-GEM (National Electricity Market Grid and Energy Management), an AI-powered operational intelligence capability co-engineered with Microsoft in less than 12 weeks. Powered by Microsoft IQ, NEM-GEM helps operators cut through thousands of signals, focus on what matters, and make faster, informed decisions, while humans remain in control. <a href="https://aka.ms/AEMO-IQ-Story" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>Learn more about AEMO’s Microsoft IQ story</strong></a>.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="turn-production-signals-into-continuous-improvement">Turn production signals into continuous improvement</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><strong>Continuous improvement in Foundry</strong> can turn your organization’s collective knowledge and live production signals into agents that can improve day over day. Teams define success, compare quality, latency, and cost per successful outcome, and approve changes before deployment. Agent Optimizer in Foundry evaluates an agent against those criteria, generates improved configurations, and ranks them so teams can deploy the best one.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Microsoft IQ carries forward the context, skills, and evaluations that prove their value across agents and workflows, so each improvement builds on the last. When changes to an agent’s configuration stop producing gains, teams can apply the same success criteria and production signals to the model itself through post-training.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><strong>Managed Training</strong> provides a simple, automated way to improve model quality and covers most customization needs. For greater control, <strong>Programmed Training</strong>—now in public preview—lets AI engineers write custom training loops, including supervised fine-tuning and reinforcement learning, for supported open-weight models, while Foundry manages the compute. The result is one path spanning simple, automated optimization through fully customized training.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Read more about continuous improvement in Tina Schuchman’s blog post, <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/continuous-improvement-comes-to-microsoft-foundry/" id="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/continuous-improvement-comes-to-microsoft-foundry/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Continuous improvement comes to Microsoft Foundry</a>.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The value of Microsoft IQ compounds when teams can reuse it across agents and put it to work in production. Teams spend less time rebuilding the same foundations and more time improving the decisions, workflows, and customer experiences that drive the business. Proven work can scale across the organization, shortening time to value, reducing duplication, and increasing trust in AI, all while the organization retains control of the knowledge and intellectual property that make it distinctive.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="explore-microsoft-iq-at-ignite-2026">Explore Microsoft IQ at Ignite 2026</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Start with one meaningful business process, one where a better decision or action can deliver measurable value. Curate your business context and expertise you already have into a plugin, connect it to your agents in Microsoft Copilot, and put Microsoft IQ to work today.</p>

		<title>Building the next generation of multimodal AI experiences with fal in Microsoft Foundry</title>
		<link>https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/azure-ai-foundry-blog/building-the-next-generation-of-multimodal-ai-experiences-with-fal-in-microsoft-/4570032</link>
					<comments>https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/azure-ai-foundry-blog/building-the-next-generation-of-multimodal-ai-experiences-with-fal-in-microsoft-/4570032#respond</comments>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Naomi Moneypenny]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Fri, 06 Nov 2026 19:08:44 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[AI + machine learning]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/?p=54939</guid>

					<description><![CDATA[<p>Microsoft and fal partner to expand video generation in Microsoft Foundry, helping developers bring creative ideas into enterprise applications.</p>
<p>The post <a href="https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/azure-ai-foundry-blog/building-the-next-generation-of-multimodal-ai-experiences-with-fal-in-microsoft-/4570032">Building the next generation of multimodal AI experiences with fal in Microsoft Foundry</a> appeared first on <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog">Microsoft Azure Blog</a>.</p>
]]></description>
										<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Microsoft and fal partner to expand video generation in Microsoft Foundry, helping developers bring creative ideas into enterprise applications.<br>Video workloads do not all start with the same input or aim for the same result. A creative team might explore several interpretations of a written brief. A retailer might turn an approved product image into a short motion concept. An application developer might combine image generation and video generation into a workflow that takes an idea from description to frame to scene. Each scenario brings different requirements for visual quality, motion, creative control, turnaround time, and cost. All of this means that a multi-model solution is necessary to realize a full vision.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Today, Microsoft and fal are helping developers find the right tools for their workloads. We are excited to announce that Kling, LTX, and MiniMax video models are rolling out to in public preview in Microsoft Foundry through our new partnership with fal. Available as a first-party Azure service, these text-to-video and image-to-video options give creators and developers more ways to utilize video generation with the experiences they want to build.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The partnership expands the choices available in Microsoft Foundry while bringing video into the broader platform developers use to build AI applications. The goal is to let developers pick the right model for each step of an experience, then connect those steps into a working application.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">“fal has seen incredible adoption this year alone with generative media across all industries from advertising to marketing to gaming. Partnering with Microsoft allows both of us to truly enable the wide creative ecosystem and ensure that all AI solutions that lean on generative media, becomes a multi-model one.” &#8211; Burkay Gur, Cofounder</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Bringing fal’s generative media focus to enterprise builders<br>fal is the full stack for generative media, built for creators and developers, with APIs for over 1500 models across image, video, audio, 3D, and generative worlds. As a truly encapsulating platform, fal provides inference, training, and infrastructure to run generative media workloads in production, while also providing innovative post trained models that pushes the boundaries on speed and quality.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">That specialization matters because generative media is both a creative and an engineering workload. A compelling demo is only the start. Production applications also need to handle real traffic, scale with demand, and balance output quality, latency, and cost.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Through this partnership, developers will be able to access a selected set of video models in Microsoft Foundry, served by fal and sourced from the companies that build them. The initial portfolio gives teams a choice of models for different creative requirements instead of relying on one model for every task, with more models planned over time.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">A portfolio for different video experiences<br>The initial portfolio spans Kling v3 Pro, LTX-2.5 Fast, LTX-2.5 Pro, MiniMax H3 Max, and MiniMax H3 Max Turbo. These models give developers different starting points for generating video from text and animating existing images. A team rapidly exploring creative concepts may prioritize speed and cost, while a team producing fewer, higher-fidelity assets may weigh output quality more heavily. Whether an application starts from text or an existing image, and what it needs in output quality and cost, also shapes the choice.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Kling: From a still image to a directed scene</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Kling v3 Pro combines cinematic visuals and fluid motion with native audio generation and custom element support. Its image-to-video capabilities give developers control over how a starting image becomes a moving scene, a fit for applications that prioritize visual direction and motion.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">LTX: One model for fast drafts, one for final output</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">For rapid iteration and previews, LTX-2.5 Fast is the speed-optimized option; for high-fidelity final output, LTX-2.5 Pro is the quality-optimized option. Both support text-to-video and image-to-video generation with synchronized audio.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">MiniMax: Another approach to video generation</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">MiniMax H3 Max and MiniMax H3 Max Turbo are post-trained variants of MiniMax H3 focused on prompt adherence, aesthetics, and inference performance. For developers, they provide additional options to evaluate against an application’s creative brief, quality requirements, and performance needs.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">“When building with video models, the details matter: how closely the output follows your prompt, how well it preserves your reference images/videos/audios and how quickly you can try another idea. Kling gives developers control over detailed reference to video generation. LTX offers fast iteration and high-quality output through its Fast and Pro variants. H3 Max brings fal&#8217;s post-training and inference optimizations to improve quality and speed. Having these models available through Microsoft Foundry gives developers more flexibility to choose what works best for the application they are building.&#8221; &#8211; Gökay Aydoğan, Creative Engineer</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Built for enterprise video workloads<br>Bringing video generation into an enterprise application requires more than choosing the right model. Teams need a platform where they can evaluate different options, build with the models that fit their requirements, and operate those experiences alongside the rest of their applications.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The initial portfolio of video models is hosted in the US, and Microsoft Foundry is the first provider to offer a US-hosted Kling endpoint. Key considerations for enterprise teams:</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Data residency: For organizations with data residency requirements, hosting location is one factor to weigh. Review each model&#8217;s documented data processing and storage terms to confirm it meets your requirements.<br>Model evaluation: Teams can assess quality, performance, and cost, then connect video generation with the other models and capabilities their applications need.<br>Production readiness: As they move toward production, they can use the evaluation, optimization, governance, security, observability, and infrastructure capabilities supported for their selected offering.<br>Pricing<br>Video generation pricing varies by model and output resolution, giving teams options to balance creative requirements with their application’s budget. The table below shows pricing in USD per minute of generated video for LTX and MiniMax models, making it easier to compare costs across the available options.</p>

		<item>
		<title>Stop treating agents as one-off projects and start running them as a continuous discipline</title>
		<link>https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/stop-treating-agents-as-one-off-projects-and-start-running-them-as-a-continuous-discipline/</link>
					<comments>https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/stop-treating-agents-as-one-off-projects-and-start-running-them-as-a-continuous-discipline/#respond</comments>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Cyril Belikoff]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Thu, 05 Nov 2026 17:00:00 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[AI + machine learning]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/?p=54831</guid>

					<description><![CDATA[<p>AI must be selected, architected, and managed as a continuous business discipline, not a series of isolated projects.</p>
<p>The post <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/stop-treating-agents-as-one-off-projects-and-start-running-them-as-a-continuous-discipline/">Stop treating agents as one-off projects and start running them as a continuous discipline</a> appeared first on <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog">Microsoft Azure Blog</a>.</p>
]]></description>
										<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Transformation begins at the line between an experiment that works and a capability the business depends on. Scaling intelligence requires production capabilities built for sustained business performance, with investments prioritized against financial thresholds, tied to measurable outcomes, instrumented for unit costs, and owned by accountable leaders. This approach manages cost, performance, governance, and reliability as adoption grows.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">A new IDC InfoBrief, sponsored by Microsoft, <a href="https://marketingassets.microsoft.com/adobe/assets/urn:aaid:aem:5d3ad527-11a7-4c8a-a95c-0b37adcbbb4b/original/as/Analyst-Report-Scaling-AI-Without-Scaling-Costs.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Scaling AI Without Scaling Costs</a>*, on how organizations are funding and governing AI, shows both the urgency of that shift and the friction holding it back. </p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://marketingassets.microsoft.com/adobe/assets/urn:aaid:aem:5d3ad527-11a7-4c8a-a95c-0b37adcbbb4b/original/as/Analyst-Report-Scaling-AI-Without-Scaling-Costs.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Read the IDC InfoBrief</a></div>
</div>



<blockquote class="wp-block-quote has-quote-default-font-size is-layout-flow wp-block-quote-is-layout-flow">
<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Nearly 90% of organizations apply financial thresholds before<br>approving AI initiatives.</p>
<cite><a href="https://marketingassets.microsoft.com/adobe/assets/urn:aaid:aem:5d3ad527-11a7-4c8a-a95c-0b37adcbbb4b/original/as/Analyst-Report-Scaling-AI-Without-Scaling-Costs.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Scaling AI Without Scaling Costs</a></cite></blockquote>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Only 17% of organizations build models to project how costs scale in production—even as about 78% prioritize cost efficiency when selecting a model. 86% expect financial results within six to 24 months—roughly one budget cycle to show something real. Together, those findings point to a new operating reality: AI must be selected, architected, and managed as a continuous business discipline, not a series of isolated projects. The market has moved from proving it works to proving it can scale economically.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="four-questions-to-answer-before-your-next-funding-decisionrun-each-candidate-through-four-questions-that-follow-a-scaled-workload-s-life-fund-it-run-it-improve-it-prove-it">Four questions to answer before your next funding decision</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph" id="four-questions-to-answer-before-your-next-funding-decisionrun-each-candidate-through-four-questions-that-follow-a-scaled-workload-s-life-fund-it-run-it-improve-it-prove-it">Run each candidate through four questions that follow a scaled workload&#8217;s life: fund it, run it, improve it, prove it.</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="1-how-can-we-predict-and-track-costs-at-scale">1. How can we predict and track costs at scale?</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">A pilot proves the idea, rarely the economics: only 17% of organizations model how costs scale in production. Before you fund, project usage at 3 times and 10 times, and make sure spend traces to an application, team, and agent. <a href="https://devblogs.microsoft.com/foundry/build-2026-foundry-models/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Compare models on cost, quality, and latency</a>, and commit steady demand to reserved capacity. If cost rises with usage while value flattens, you&#8217;ve found an architecture problem while it&#8217;s still cheap to fix.</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="2-how-can-we-optimize-model-costs-for-every-request">2. How can we optimize model costs for every request?</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">One successful outcome can take a dozen model requests, so what matters is cost per outcome, not price per token. Ask:</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item">Is routine work like classification paying frontier-model prices?</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Are the same instructions reprocessed on every turn?</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Is work that can wait running at a lower-cost tier?</li>
</ul>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The goal is the right model for every request, with evaluation proving quality held.</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="3-how-can-we-continuously-improve-agent-performance-and-costs">3. How can we continuously improve agent performance and costs?</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Retrieved documents, tool definitions, instructions, and history are often an agent&#8217;s largest cost and a common cause of weak answers. Check:</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item">Does the agent retrieve the relevant passage or the whole document?</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Does it carry every tool description on every turn?</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Is the same procedure copied into every agent?</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Does it replay full transcripts instead of remembering what matters?</li>
</ul>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/the-economics-of-agent-optimization-context-engineering-for-enterprise-ai-agents/">Trimming what the model doesn&#8217;t need lowers cost without hurting quality.</a></p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="4-how-can-we-control-spending-and-measure-roi">4. How can we control spending and measure ROI?</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">A budget alert reports the fire after it starts; a runaway agent needs a circuit breaker on every request. Cost must tie to the outcome the business pays for, such as a resolved case, so leaders can optimize, scale, or retire an agent. The business owner also needs an evaluation suite, cost and latency budgets, a rollback path, and regular reviews. With 86% of organizations expecting financial results within six to 24 months, that evidence has about one budget cycle to appear.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph" id="all-four-answers-depend-on-evidence-captured-where-ai-runs-per-request-agent-and-outcome-if-each-team-builds-it-by-hand-the-discipline-won-t-survive-the-tenth-agent-it-has-to-come-from-the-platform-why-microsoft-and-how-microsoft-foundry-helps"><strong>All four answers depend on evidence captured where AI runs:</strong> per request, agent, and outcome. If each team builds it by hand, the discipline won&#8217;t survive the tenth agent. It has to come from the platform.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="all-four-answers-depend-on-evidence-captured-where-ai-runs-per-request-agent-and-outcome-if-each-team-builds-it-by-hand-the-discipline-won-t-survive-the-tenth-agent-it-has-to-come-from-the-platform-why-microsoft-and-how-microsoft-foundry-helps">How Microsoft Foundry supports continuous cost optimization</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">When customers ask for a few practical ways to scale AI while controlling costs, I recommend: </p>



<ol class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item">Use model routing across the over 11,000 models available in <a href="https://ai.azure.com/home" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Foundry</a>.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Implement <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/ai/microsoft-iq" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft IQ</a> to improve relevance and reduce costs.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Use the plan, control, and optimize capabilities described below to strengthen operations across the organization.</li>
</ol>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://ai.azure.com/home" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Foundry</a> is the enterprise platform to build, ground, and govern AI apps and agents at scale. It brings together your full agent lifecycle with open development, built-in intelligence, and consistent security, compliance, and policy controls across every agent. Think of it as a repeatable production line rather than a set of one-off builds, where the same controls, evidence, and economics apply to the tenth agent as to the first.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">This is what makes the operating loop practical at scale. Across the loop:</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><strong>Plan:</strong> Compare models on cost, quality, and performance before committing to an architecture, and match predictable baseline demand to reserved capacity while keeping the flexibility to burst. The decisions that set your production cost curve get made with data rather than intuition.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><strong>Control</strong>: <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/agent-factory-top-5-agent-observability-best-practices-for-reliable-ai/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Observability</a> that reports cost alongside quality, latency, and safety in one view, so the economics of a workload are visible while it&#8217;s running rather than reconstructed at quarter-end. That&#8217;s what makes cost per outcome a number you can put in front of your CFO.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><strong>Optimize:</strong> Model routing that sends each request to the smallest model that can serve it, an intelligence layer that provides context and retrieval centrally and addresses a common source of avoidable token spend, and evaluation tooling that catches quality regression when you make a cost decision—because a cheaper system that quietly degrades isn&#8217;t a saving.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The value compounds with volume. When every agent inherits the same grounding, governance, and cost telemetry from the platform, scaling from one use case to 20 doesn&#8217;t mean rebuilding the discipline 20 times. What <a href="https://ai.azure.com/home" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Foundry</a> does is make sure the evidence exists when the funding conversation comes around, and the levers are within reach when you need them.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="next-steps-for-optimizing-ai-agent-costs-and-roi">Next steps for optimizing AI agent costs and ROI</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Scaling AI isn&#8217;t a bigger experiment. It&#8217;s a different discipline: a shared threshold, a unit cost, a value curve that outruns the cost curve, and an owner whose name is on the result.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Go deeper with <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/tag/the-economics-of-agent-optimization/">our four-part blog series on The Economics of Agent Optimization</a> in Microsoft Foundry, which takes each of these questions from principle to implementation:</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item"><a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/the-economics-of-agent-optimization-from-pilots-to-measurable-returns/">AI cost management: From AI pilots to measurable ROI</a></li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/the-economics-of-agent-optimization-four-ways-to-lower-the-cost/">AI cost optimization: How to lower AI spend</a></li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/the-economics-of-agent-optimization-context-engineering-for-enterprise-ai-agents/">AI agent optimization: How context engineering lowers AI costs</a></li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/the-economics-of-agent-optimization-how-ai-agent-governance-controls-cost-and-proves-roi/">AI agent governance: How to measure AI value and ROI</a></li>
</ul>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Then join us at <a href="https://ignite.microsoft.com/en-US/home">Microsoft Ignite</a>, November 17–20 in San Francisco or online, to see it in action and go hands-on with the team that built it.</p>

		<item>
		<title>Designing Azure silicon on Azure silicon: EDA workloads on Cobalt 200</title>
		<link>https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/designing-azure-silicon-on-azure-silicon-eda-workloads-on-cobalt-200/</link>
					<comments>https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/designing-azure-silicon-on-azure-silicon-eda-workloads-on-cobalt-200/#respond</comments>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Selim Bilgin]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Thu, 05 Nov 2026 16:00:00 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Compute]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/?p=54813</guid>

					<description><![CDATA[<p>Discover how Microsoft is accelerating electronic design automation (EDA) workloads with Azure Cobalt 200 VMs. Explore early performance results demonstrating faster simulation, verification, and static timing analysis, along with the benefits of scalable cloud infrastructure for silicon design and development.</p>
<p>The post <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/designing-azure-silicon-on-azure-silicon-eda-workloads-on-cobalt-200/">Designing Azure silicon on Azure silicon: EDA workloads on Cobalt 200</a> appeared first on <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog">Microsoft Azure Blog</a>.</p>
]]></description>
										<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<div class="wp-block-bloginabox-theme-post-alert" role="alert" aria-live="polite" data-bi-an="alert">
	<div class="alert-content">
		Azure Cobalt 200 VMs is now generally available. <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/azure-cobalt-200-vms-now-available-proven-on-real-workloads-and-built-for-the-agentic-era/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Read more</a>.	</div>
</div>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Every Azure chip Microsoft has built started as electronic design automation (EDA) jobs used to design, test, and validate silicon before it is manufactured. This includes simulation sweeps that run for days, verification regressions that fan out across thousands of cores overnight, and physical signoff runs that use hundreds of gigabytes of memory for a single block. Together, these workloads account for hundreds of millions of compute jobs each year. Today we’re sharing early results from our next step in that evolution: running EDA workloads on <a href="https://aka.ms/Cobalt200VMs-GA" id="https://aka.ms/Cobalt200VMs-GA" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>Azure Cobalt 200</strong></a>, Azure’s custom compute infrastructure built on the Arm Neoverse CSS V3. In our silicon design environment, the evaluated EDA workloads ran with <strong>1.30x to 4.15x more performance</strong> than on Cobalt 100 across a range of testing scenarios, from simulation to verification to static timing analysis.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://aka.ms/Cobalt200VMs-GA" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer noreferrer noopener">Learn more about Azure Cobalt 200 VMs</a></div>
</div>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Traditionally, EDA workloads ran on fixed, on-premise x86 server capacity, sized for peak demand with periods of underutilization. The shift to cloud-based EDA on Microsoft Azure offered a new level of flexibility, scale, and reliability on compute-intensive silicon design workloads. Using <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/virtual-machines/sizes/cobalt-overview" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Azure Cobalt-based virtual machine</a> (VM) instances, silicon engineering teams at Microsoft are experiencing significant performance and efficiency improvements on EDA workloads that Azure customers are seeing across many workloads—optimizing the performance and speed of designing increasingly sophisticated silicon for increasingly complex cloud systems.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">In this blog, we highlight results from Cadence Xcelium and Cadence Spectre, Synopsys PrimeTime and Synopsys VCS, and Siemens Calibre tools. Tools enabled on Cobalt 100 ran unmodified in our Cobalt 200 evaluation, allowing us to focus on performance testing rather than functionality.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="why-cobalt-200-suits-eda">Why Cobalt 200 suits EDA</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">EDA spans workloads with very different compute requirements:</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Circuit and analog simulation</strong> — optimized for highly parallel workloads, with sensitivity to memory behavior.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Functional and formal verification</strong> — throughput-driven, with large numbers of independent jobs running concurrently.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Implementation and signoff</strong> — memory-intensive and bandwidth-sensitive, with long-running jobs.</li>
</ul>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">For computer-aided design (CAD) managers, predictable per-core performance is essential to planning overnight regressions. Per-core performance measured on a lightly loaded VM does not necessarily hold as concurrency increases. Verification farms need predictable throughput when VMs are heavily loaded.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Cobalt 200 combines physical cores without the overhead of hyperthreading, dedicated 3MB L2 cache per core, and high provisioned memory bandwidth to support concurrent workloads. These core and memory architecture characteristics support strong per-core performance at high utilization. </p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="what-we-measured">What we measured</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">We tested Cobalt 200 with workloads from several stages of Microsoft’s silicon development flow, including RTL and gate-level simulation, static timing, physical verification, and analog simulation, comparing job completion time with Cobalt 100 and selected x86 Azure VMs.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">All tested VMs were configured with the maximum number of cores possible for high throughput<sup> 5</sup>, multiple jobs were concurrently executed on the VM to validate the performance under high load conditions. Throughput is normalized per logical core. For the Cobalt 100, Cobalt 200, and hx176rs configurations, logical cores are also physical cores. </p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="consolidated-eda-workload-results">Consolidated EDA workload results</h2>



<figure class="wp-block-table is-style-stripes"><table class="has-fixed-layout"><thead><tr><th>Workload</th><th>EDA tool</th><th>Measure</th><th>Cobalt 200 result</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><strong>Digital simulation</strong></td><td>Cadence Xcelium<sup>1</sup></td><td>Peak throughput per logical core</td><td><strong>More than 40% higher</strong> than any other tested system</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Digital simulation</strong></td><td>Synopsys VCS<sup>1</sup></td><td>Peak throughput per logical core</td><td><strong>More than 40% higher</strong> than any other tested system</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Analog and mixed-signal simulation</strong></td><td>Cadence Spectre<sup>2</sup></td><td>Throughput per logical core</td><td><strong>38% higher</strong> generation over generation; exceeded tested x86 Azure VMs</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Physical verification</strong></td><td>Siemens Calibre<sup>3</sup> DRC</td><td>Wall time / relative performance</td><td><strong>23% lower</strong> wall time; 1.30× relative performance versus Cobalt 100</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Static timing analysis</strong></td><td>Synopsys PrimeTime<sup>4</sup></td><td>Peak throughput per logical core</td><td><strong>2.0×</strong> Cobalt 100; exceeded tested x86 Azure VMs</td></tr></tbody></table></figure>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="digital-simulation">Digital simulation</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Cadence Xcelium and Synopsys VCS are tools used to simulate and verify digital designs. RTL simulation is central to functional verification and can consume significant compute capacity as teams run large regression suites. When testing RTL workloads using both simulators, optimally loaded Cobalt 200 systems delivered significantly greater peak throughput than the previous generation. For the workload tested on Xcelium and VCS, Cobalt 200 delivered more than <strong>40% higher throughput per logical core than any other system we tested</strong>.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Throughput is normalized per logical core to account for the VMs’ different core counts.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Cobalt 200 was optimized for EDA workloads through increased cache size and improved memory bandwidth per core. Our results demonstrate that Cobalt 200 design optimizations generate significantly improved performance for logic simulation workloads. </p>



<figure data-wp-context="{&quot;imageId&quot;:&quot;6afcf4d83c428&quot;}" data-wp-interactive="core/image" data-wp-key="6afcf4d83c428" class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-large wp-lightbox-container"><img decoding="async" width="1024" height="575" data-wp-class--hide="state.isContentHidden" data-wp-class--show="state.isContentVisible" data-wp-init="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on--click="actions.showLightbox" data-wp-on--load="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on-window--resize="callbacks.setButtonStyles" src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/Cadence-Xcelium-RTLSimulation-Throughput-1-1024x575.jpg" alt="Bar chart comparing Cadence Xcelium RTL simulation throughput, normalized to Azure Cobalt 100. <h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="analog-and-mixed-signal-simulation">Analog and mixed signal simulation</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Cadence Spectre is used for analog and mixed-signal circuit simulation. Analog and mixed-signal circuit simulation workloads differ from RTL simulation and are dominated by multithreaded performance. We tested Cadence Spectre on Cobalt 200 and observed <strong>38% higher throughput per logical core </strong>generation over generation, and delivered 11-52% higher throughput per logical core compared with all other platforms tested. </p>



<figure data-wp-context="{&quot;imageId&quot;:&quot;6afcf4d83da57&quot;}" data-wp-interactive="core/image" data-wp-key="6afcf4d83da57" class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-large wp-lightbox-container"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" width="1024" height="570" data-wp-class--hide="state.isContentHidden" data-wp-class--show="state.isContentVisible" data-wp-init="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on--click="actions.showLightbox" data-wp-on--load="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on-window--resize="callbacks.setButtonStyles" src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/Cadence-Spectre-Analog-Simulation-Throughput-1-1024x570.jpg" alt="Bar chart comparing Cadence Spectre analog simulation throughput, normalized to Azure Cobalt 100. <p class="wp-block-paragraph">This result shows meaningful progress between Cobalt generations. </p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="physical-verification">Physical verification</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Siemens Calibre DRC checks a design’s physical layout against manufacturing design rules before tapeout.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">For the Calibre workload we tested DRC against an IP Macro with 16 cores per job, Cobalt 200 completed the run in approximately 23% lower wall time than Cobalt 100, equivalent to 1.30x relative performance. It also outperformed the selected x86 Azure VMs at the same per-job core count.</p>

<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="static-timing-analysis-on-synopsys-primetime">Static timing analysis on Synopsys PrimeTime</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Synopsys PrimeTime is used for static timing analysis to verify that a design meets its timing requirements. For the PrimeTime workload tested with 16 cores per job, Cobalt 200 delivered 2x the peak throughput per logical core of Cobalt 100 and exceeded the selected x86 Azure VMs. Figure 5 compares each VM platform’s highest measured throughput value, normalized to Cobalt 100.</p>

<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="more-than-just-an-soc-platform-level-advantages-to-cobalt-200">More than just an SoC: Platform-level advantages to Cobalt 200</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">A higher-performance compute SoC only helps if the rest of the platform scales its performance as well. EDA workloads depend on high-bandwidth and low-latency storage and data movement, and demand varies throughout a silicon program. Verification activity can spike before a milestone, while physical verification and signoff activity increase toward tapeout. Demand can fall sharply once those milestones are completed.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Running on Azure pairs Cobalt 200 compute with:</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Elastic capacity</strong>: provisioning compute capacity for a regression campaign or signoff push, then release it as demand falls. Size for the workload, not the annual peak.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>High-performance storage: </strong>supporting the data demands of large design databases, waveform dumps, and physical verification runs.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Global capacity</strong>: aligning deployments with engineering locations, data residency requirements, and regional availability.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Adjacency to Azure AI services</strong>: As agentic and AI-assisted design workflows evolve, proximity between AI inference services and Cobalt 200 compute creates opportunities for tighter integration of AI-powered reasoning with EDA execution. This <strong>reduces</strong> data movement overhead, accelerates iterative design and verification cycles, and enables AI agents to orchestrate complex engineering workflows more efficiently.</li>
</ul>



<blockquote class="wp-block-quote has-quote-default-font-size is-layout-flow wp-block-quote-is-layout-flow">
<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Microsoft is a key partner for Cadence, and our collaboration on Cobalt spans multiple generations of Azure silicon. With Cadence EDA workflows now qualified on Cobalt 200, we are extending that collaboration to the latest generation of Azure infrastructure and giving semiconductor teams greater flexibility in how they access and scale compute for demanding design and verification workloads.</p>
<cite><strong>—Paul Cunningham, Sr. VP &amp; GM, System Verification Group, Cadence</strong></cite></blockquote>



<blockquote class="wp-block-quote has-quote-default-font-size is-layout-flow wp-block-quote-is-layout-flow">
<p class="wp-block-paragraph">As semiconductor designs grow increasingly complex, engineering teams face mounting pressure to accelerate silicon development while managing growing compute demands. Executing Siemens EDA solutions on Microsoft Azure Cobalt 200 provides an efficient, scalable platform for demanding verification and signoff workloads. Our collaboration with Microsoft demonstrates the value of matching the right workloads to the right compute architecture to advance silicon innovation.</p>
<cite><strong>—Craig Johnson, VP of Cloud Solutions, Siemens EDA</strong></cite></blockquote>



<blockquote class="wp-block-quote has-quote-default-font-size is-layout-flow wp-block-quote-is-layout-flow">
<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Our longstanding partnership with Microsoft is grounded in a shared commitment to help engineering teams tackle increasingly complex design challenges. We congratulate Microsoft on the impressive performance of Cobalt 200, developed using Synopsys’ comprehensive EDA flow spanning verification, implementation and signoff, together with HAPS hardware prototyping and Synopsys IP. We’re excited to see Cobalt 200 delivering meaningful performance gains generation-on-generation, including more than 40% faster runtimes with Synopsys VCS and 2x per-core throughput with Synopsys PrimeTime. We look forward to continuing our collaboration to help engineering teams advance the next generation of AI silicon.</p>
<cite><strong>—Sanjay Bali, Senior Vice President of EDA Product Management and Strategy at Synopsys</strong></cite></blockquote>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="designing-next-generation-cobalt-with-cobalt-200">Designing Next-Generation Cobalt with Cobalt 200</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">There’s a virtuous cycle between silicon development and EDA performance: EDA tools helped bring Cobalt 200 to life, and now Cobalt 200 is accelerating those same design and verification workloads for our next-generation Cobalt products. By observing how EDA workloads perform at scale, we gain insights that help shape future generations of Cobalt and Azure compute infrastructure, with each generation helping design and improve the next. </p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="what-comes-next">What comes next</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Our early tests show significant progress for EDA on Cobalt 200. Existing Arm-enabled tools ran without workflow changes, and the tested workloads performed competitively against the selected x86 Azure VMs. Xcelium and PrimeTime showed a particularly large improvement over Cobalt 100. Spectre and Calibre which were strong performers on Cobalt 100 also improved generation over generation, and we are working with our EDA partners on further tuning of multiple tools.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">We are broadening workload coverage, validating results on a greater scale, and continuing to collaborate with our EDA partners as Cobalt 200 enters general availability.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">If you&#8217;re evaluating cloud infrastructure for semiconductor design, we invite you to test your EDA workflows on <a href="https://aka.ms/Cobalt200VMs-GA" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Azure Cobalt 200 VMs</a>.</p>

<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="run-next-generation-ai-workloads-with-azure-cobalt-200">Run next-generation AI workloads with Azure Cobalt 200</h2>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-layout-flex wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
